No schema field, DDL column, §7.1.3 message kind or field, store slice or §6 payload shape changed, `SCHEDULER_PROTOCOL_VERSION` is unchanged at 2, and **no §9.2 migration was added**._ **(1) The gap.** All eight §12 phases are complete, so this is defect closure against §9.5 — "the bounce builder reconstructs the full graph … inside the offline context". `renderSegments` connected every voice straight to a bare `GainNode` on `destination`, so a bounce was a DRY MIX: no level, no pan, no send, no insert and no §7.8 mixer automation reached any bounced file, and `bounceTrack`'s "post-insert, pre-master" comment described a channel the offline context did not have. Voice-level §6 sound design DID render — the pool was real, `prepareVoiceWorklets` was already called, and `mirroredTrim` was already shared — which is why nothing about a bounced file looked broken. It simply was not the mix. Measured: with a level, a pan, a send, a track insert, a master lane and a stem each applied in turn, all eight renders came back byte-identical. **(2) The offline graph is the LIVE one, built by the live factories on the offline context.** `MixerGraph`, `createChannelStrip`, `createInsert`, `createAudioBridge` and `VoicePool` all already took a `BaseAudioContext` rather than an `AudioContext`, so §9.5's "reconstructs the full graph" is satisfied by CONSTRUCTING it rather than by describing it a second time. ⚑ **This is the whole of the design decision, and it is not a preference.** Two builders that can disagree about §5.2 is how this defect would come back — the §14 (ao) rule about one expansion of the §7.9 playlist, one layer down. A parallel offline builder would have had to restate the insert chain's serial wiring, the post-fader send taps, the equal-power pan, the §8.5.6 fader law, the §5.7.3 PDC leg and the §5.2 return topology, and would have drifted from the live one at the first §5.7 effect added to either. ⚑ **A render therefore carries §5.2 stages 1 to 10**: the §5.4 pool, the pad strip (its §6 mixer seeded first, then the §4.2 store's own strip where it has one), the track group with its inserts and sends, the four returns, the master bus, the master inserts, and `destination`. ⚑ **`prepareWorkletEffects` is now called beside `prepareVoiceWorklets`.** A §5.7 `reverb`, `limiter` or `multibandComp` in any slot is an `AudioWorkletNode`, which THROWS on a context that has never registered `dsp-effect` — so the moment inserts entered the render, the second registration became mandatory rather than an optimisation. **(3) Two stages are deliberately absent, and neither is a mix decision.** ⚑ **The §5.9 monitor bus.** §5.9 merges the metronome and Browser-mode auditioning into `destination` PAST the master inserts precisely so they are not part of the programme material; a click in a mixdown is a fault in any host. No `Metronome` and no `PreviewChannel` is built, so the bus `MixerGraph` constructs renders silence at the cost of one gain node. ⚑ **The §5.8 metering taps.** They exist to write a SAB a rAF loop reads, and an offline render has neither. They contribute no signal either way — their sinks sit at zero gain — so omitting them changes the audio not at all and saves a worklet per strip. **(4) The three §9.5 variants are three different spans, and `bouncePlan.bounceIncludesChannel` is the one place that says which.** ⚑ **A stem is "post-insert, pre-master", and that means the master STRIP is not applied — not that the graph is rewired.** The bus stays the unity pass-through `createChannelStrip` builds, so the topology is byte-identical to every other bounce and "pre-master" costs one predicate rather than a second wiring path. ⚑ **The static pass and the §7.8 automation pass read the SAME predicate.** A stem whose fader sat at unity while a master lane rode it for the whole render would be a stem in name only, and one rule stated twice is how that happens. ⚑ **The RETURN channels stay in a stem, and this is the one judgement rather than a reading.** A stem set has to sum back to what the master bus was fed, or it is not a set of stems; drop the returns and every send effect the user heard vanishes from the sum. Each stem carries only the return signal its own sends drove, so summing them reconstructs stage 8's input rather than counting one shared reverb once per track. The sum is exact only for a LINEAR return chain — a compressor on a return responds to the whole bus, not to one track's share of it — which is true of stems in every host and is recorded rather than worked around. ⚑ **Resample-to-pad renders the SAME graph as a sequence bounce, master inserts included.** §8.5.8's Looper is the app's other resampler and it taps `graph.master.meterPoint`, the master strip's OUTPUT; §9.5 lists resampling beside the bounces rather than beside the stem. Resampling in BangerBox therefore means one thing everywhere — what came out of the master bus — and the in-repo precedent rule (§13.6) settles it rather than taste. The cost is the familiar one, that a resampled pad played back through the master chain is processed by it twice, and the answer is the familiar one: bypass or trim the master chain first, which is a choice the user can see where a silently different render is not. ⚑ **Mute and solo are honoured, and solo is evaluated against the WHOLE mixer even when the render is narrower.** `computeEffectiveMutes` is the one §5.2 rule; judging solo-in-place on a subset would make the stem of a soloed track silence itself. **(5) The anchor a ramp starts from is the param's own TIMELINE, not a clock read (§4.3).** `rampParamLinear` did `setValueAtTime(param.value, ctxTime)`, and `param.value` reports the value NOW. ⚑ **Offline that is not merely stale, it is constant**: an `OfflineAudioContext` computes nothing until `startRendering()`, so every one of the ~80 automation windows in a two-second bar would have read the same pre-render number and each window would have jumped the fader back to it — a 40 Hz sawtooth on the master, not an automation ramp. ⚑ **Live it was stale by up to `LOOKAHEAD_MS`**, because §7.8 schedules ahead of the playhead: each window's ramp started from the value standing 100 ms earlier, flattening the run-up between windows. `cancelAndHoldAtTime(ctxTime)` asks the timeline what the contour holds AT the ramp's start, which is what the anchor always meant; it is the method `voiceEnvelope.ts` already uses for a param that keeps sounding, and `ramps.ts`'s own comment already named it as the right tool for that case. Firefox's missing implementation is supplied by the issue-#109 polyfill installed at context creation. ⚑ **Cancelling from `ctxTime` is the other half and is deliberate**: a live gesture now supersedes automation already queued ahead of the playhead, instead of being overwritten by it a moment later. **(6) One §7.8 emission rule and one lane-selection rule, both now shared.** `automationRampForWindow` was already "the only implementation of the emission rule" (§14 (am)); `SchedulerCore.automatedTargets` and `SchedulerCore.effectivePoints` were not, and a render needs both. They move into `automation.ts` as `automatedTargets` and `laneForTarget`, and the core calls them. ⚑ **A render walks each segment in `SCHEDULER_INTERVAL_MS` windows — the interval at which §7.1.4 actually reaches the graph.** Rendering the authored curve directly would be a second implementation of §7.8's three curve shapes AND a mix the user has never heard: the staircase is what live playback sounds like, and a bounce is of what was played. ⚑ **§7.8's two scopes are read exactly as `SchedulerCore` reads them.** A sequence lane loops with the pattern and is sampled at the segment's own sequence tick; a track lane spans the arrangement and, in a SONG render, is sampled at the absolute song tick. A sequence render samples both at the sequence tick, because there the pattern IS the arrangement. ⚑ **`resolveEffectivePoints` is no longer exported.** Its only production caller is `laneForTarget` in its own module, and the rule is now testable through that; keeping the export would have meant a `check:orphans` allowlist entry for a helper reachable through a pure function beside it. **(7) What a render cannot see, and does not guess.** ⚑ **A bounce is of COMMITTED state, and the rule the handover recorded for `programWithLiveGestures` now covers the mixer too.** A §4.1 gesture in flight lives on the transient channel and has not been committed, so a fader held down while Bounce is tapped renders at the value the project holds. The rule did not change when the mixer entered the render; it gained a second surface, for the same reason — a knob nobody has let go of is not part of the arrangement, and a file that cannot be reproduced from the saved project is worse than one that is a moment out of date. ⚑ **A §10.2 pitch bend is never applied.** It is a performance gesture on sounding voices, it persists nowhere, and §10.2 places it on the voice pool rather than in the store. ⚑ **A §7.8 lane addressing a PER-VOICE §6 parameter is not applied, and the bounce's bridge is given no voice pool to make that explicit.** `voicePool.applyPadParam` ramps the voices sounding NOW, and offline every voice of the whole render exists from the moment it is built — so "sounding now" is the entire span at once, and the write would land on the same `AudioParam` the voice's own §6 contour already occupies. §14 (x) settled that a scheduled contour and a live retune may never share one param. Channel-scope program addresses — a pad's amp and pan — are unaffected, because they resolve to the pad STRIP, which the render has. Filed as issue #138 rather than half-rendered here. ⚑ **The §5.7.9 warp worklet IS part of the arrangement**, and was already handled; the §5.7 worklet effects now are too. **(8) A bounce is now 3 dB quieter per channel than it used to be, and that is the fix rather than a side effect.** A mono sample reaching `destination` through a bare gain stayed mono; through the §5.2 pad strip it passes an equal-power `StereoPannerNode` at centre, which is −3 dB into each of two channels at the same total power. Measured: 0.13638 RMS before, 0.09644 after, a ratio of exactly 1/√2. The new number is what the live engine produces for the same hit, because live playback goes through that same panner — so the bounce now matches what the user hears, which is the whole complaint. **(9) What the tests now pin.** 1874 unit tests, up from 1852, across 173 files. `bouncePlan.test.ts` drives the pure half — which strips each variant covers, the emission resolution, contiguous windows, the two §7.8 scopes across a song entry boundary, and the override rule — and `automation.test.ts` gains `laneForTarget` and `automatedTargets` in place of the `resolveEffectivePoints` block they subsume. `graph.test.ts` gains the §7.8 slot-address block the review's first finding is about, and `audioBridge.test.ts`'s insert assertion now pins the SHAPE of the chain rather than its length. **Every regression test was proven against the code it was written to catch**, by six mutations: a `bounceIncludesChannel` that admits everything (2 failures), a valueTick that ignores the song track scope (1), a `laneForTarget` that ignores which sequence owns a lane (2, one on each side), the old stale ramp anchor (3), the compacted 0-based insert chain (3), and a `applyInserts` that filters empty slots out again (1). **(10) Measured in a real browser** (spec §13.5), not inferred: 13/13 driver checks at port 5344 and the full §11.4 smoke at 5342/5343, **70/70 steps** (up from 69), no console errors. `bounceMixProof` renders nine bounces of one bar carrying four hits of a 1 kHz tone and reads each back from `/bounces/` over real OPFS, so every number is what is in the file the user gets. A −12 dB track fader rendered at ×0.251 of unity; a hard-right pan left 0.00000 RMS in the left channel and raised the right one by √2; a 100 Hz lowpass insert left ×0.018 of a 1 kHz tone; an open send returned 0.1508 RMS into a beat gap that is silent in every other render; a master lane climbed 0.0013 → 0.0485 across the bar where the unautomated first beat sits at 0.1283; an `insert:track:<id>:slot1.cutoff` lane on an `exp` curve climbed 0.0008 → 0.1330 as the filter opened from 60 Hz to 12 kHz; and a master strip cut to −42 dB and lowpassed left ×0.0001 of the mix while the STEM of the same project rendered at ×1.000 of the pre-master mix and kept its own send return. **Against the unfixed build every render was identical at 0.13638 RMS**, and the 12 checks that existed at that point failed 9 of 12; the three that passed are the anti-over-correction guards — a dry mix still sounds, a dry mix has a silent beat gap, and a stem trivially matches a mix when neither renders the master strip, which is why the mastered-mix companion reading exists. The thirteenth check, the insert-parameter lane, was added by the review and was measured separately against the compacted insert chain rather than against the dry-mix build (item (12)). **(12) What the review caught, and what each answer is.** Two findings, both fixed, and the first is a defect this work is what made reachable. ⚑ **A §7.8 `slotN` address means the same thing to the graph as it does to the store.** `insertParamPath` numbers a slot 1-based over the §4.2 slot ARRAY — `InsertPanel` builds it as `slotIndex + 1` — while `applyInserts` compacted the empty slots out and `ChannelHandle.setInsertParam` indexed that compacted list 0-based. So on a strip with one delay, `insert:…:slot1.time` reached `handles[1]`, which is undefined, and nothing happened; with a filter in slot 1 and a delay in slot 2, `slot1.mix` moved the DELAY's mix. It is pre-existing, and it was invisible because a LIVE gesture is saved by the §4.3 sync layer: `mixerSync` diffs on `inserts` identity and rebuilds the whole chain at the commit, so a mis-addressed transient write is overwritten a moment later by a correct rebuild. A render has no rebuild to save it, so routing §7.8 automation into the offline graph is what made the defect audible. The chain is now the STORE's slot list — one handle per §4.2 slot, `null` where the slot is empty, and only the non-null entries wired in order — so the address cannot mean two things. `setInsertTempo` and `insertLatencySamples` skip the empty slots; the wiring, the disposal and the §5.7.3 PDC total are unchanged for a strip with no gaps. **Measured**: an `insert:…:slot1.cutoff` lane sweeping 60 Hz to 12 kHz rendered 0.0008 → 0.1330 RMS across the bar; against the compacted chain the same lane rendered 0.0005 → 0.0005, the filter sitting at its stored 60 Hz for the whole bar. ⚑ **The §7.1.3 `rampEnd` is deliberately not consumed, and `applyAutomation` now says so rather than dropping it silently.** Gliding across the whole window instead of dezippering to the value is not expressible for half the targets: pan and every §5.7 effect core ramp with `setTargetAtTime`, which has no arrival time at all — its shape is a settle, not a ramp. Making an automation window the one writer to these params with a different §4.3 shape, for half of them only, would be worse than arriving early, and `PARAM_RAMP_MS` (10 ms) is shorter than `SCHEDULER_INTERVAL_MS` (25 ms), so the param holds the window's OWN value for the remainder rather than lagging it. The parameter stays in the signature because §7.1.3 carries it and `engine.ts` passes it. What was genuinely wrong was a comment: `bouncePlan.test.ts` justified the contiguity assertion by saying the param "interpolates continuously", which it does not — the property that assertion actually pins is that no tick of a segment is left unwritten or written twice, and it now says that. **(11) Scope and honest limits.** ⚑ **A §7.8 lane on a §6 sound-design parameter still renders as nothing** — item (7), filed as issue #138. ⚑ **A pad channel is seeded from the §6 payload and then overwritten by the §4.2 store where a strip exists**, which is the §1.3 #16 order; for a pad of a program that is not the active one, `programSync` has published no strip and the payload is the only value there is. That is moot until issue #133 makes a pad strip persist at all. ⚑ **Nothing bounds the number of automation events a long song schedules.** A render's memory is bounded by the `OfflineAudioContext` buffer long before its event count matters — a §7.9 playlist at the `MAX_SONG_SEGMENTS` cap is over 55 hours, which is ~76 GB of float samples — so the browser's own allocation failure is the limit, exactly as it was before this work. No new guard was invented for a case the existing one already dominates. ⚑ **`rampParamLinear`'s new anchor changes live behaviour**, and deliberately: automation ramps now start from where the contour actually is rather than from a reading up to 100 ms old. The full unit suite and all 70 smoke steps are green on it, and one existing assertion in `paramGuards.test.ts` was corrected rather than added to, because it pinned the scheduling call rather than the behaviour. ⚑ **A §7.8 lane can still address a slot the §1.3.1 limit forbids**, because `addInsert` never enforced it (issue #135) — the grammar bounds a slot at 8 and a ninth insert is unaddressable, exactly as that issue records. Nothing here changes it; what changed is that slots 1 to 8 now mean what they say. ⚑ **A stem still renders through the master BUS**, at unity with no inserts, rather than tapping the track strip directly. That is what makes the topology identical across variants; it costs three pass-through gain nodes and a `StereoPannerNode` at centre, none of which changes the signal.

- **2026-09-04 (aq)** — _Sequence mode plays ONE sequence, and a §7.7 live erase reaches only that one (§7.7, §7.9). Closes issue #132. Adds one private predicate in `SchedulerCore` and one §11.4 probe seam with the smoke step that drives it. No schema field, DDL column, §7.1.3 message kind or field, store slice or §6 payload shape changed, `SCHEDULER_PROTOCOL_VERSION` is unchanged at 2, and **no §9.2 migration was added**._ **(1) The gap.** All eight §12 phases are complete, so this is defect closure in `src/core/sequencer/` again, and it is the mirror image of issue #94: there song mode omitted what sequence mode did, and here sequence mode omits the one thing song mode does. `scheduleSequence` iterated the whole track map with no `sequenceId` filter where `emitSongPass` has `track.sequenceId !== segment.sequenceId`, so every sequence in the project sounded at once and §8.5.12's ordered playlist was indistinguishable in sound from simply pressing play. The second half costs data rather than sound: a pad held over Erase took its notes out of every sequence carrying that pad, and `result.erased` reaches the store through `removeEvents`, so the deletion persists. **(2) The filter belongs in the SCHEDULE path, and the sender ships the whole project.** ⚑ **The worker holds every track because song mode selects a different one per SEGMENT.** A filter in `sequencerSync` would empty song mode outright, which is the decisive argument against the other side of the wire and the reason this is not a matter of taste. ⚑ **§7.1.3 makes `eventsDiff` "incremental — never full re-sends during playback".** A sender-side filter turns every switch of active sequence into exactly that forbidden shape: delete every track of the sequence being left, upsert every track of the one being entered, while the transport rolls. ⚑ **A track whose sequence becomes active LATER is already right at the moment the transport reaches it.** That is the property the worker-side filter buys and the sender-side one destroys. Because the map holds the project, a switch sends `sequenceMeta` and nothing else, and the very next wake schedules the new pattern; a sender-side filter would instead leave a window between the switch and the arrival of that sequence's diffs in which the worker holds no events for the pattern it has just been told to play, and would schedule silence into it. The two filters are therefore ONE rule stated in the two places that schedule — each mode selects the sequence it is playing — rather than two rules that can disagree, which is how this defect survived. ⚑ **A `null` active sequence matches nothing, so nothing sounds.** §7.1.4 does not say, and the permissive reading is precisely what made every track sound. `tracksOfSequence` already answers the same question with the empty list for the §8.5.1 track panel, and it matters more than a display default because §7.7's erase deletes what it sweeps: the safe reading of "no sequence selected" is that no track is armed, not that every track is. It is unreachable in a loaded project in any case — `hydrate` activates the first sequence row and `deleteSequence` refuses to remove the last. ⚑ **Note repeat and the arpeggiator are deliberately NOT filtered.** A §7.3 hit carries the track the input layer chose (issue #25), and `usePadTrigger` already scopes that to the active sequence; filtering again in the worker would second-guess a live gesture the user is making now, and song mode does not filter them either. **(3) Not fixed here, and now filed: a DELETED track keeps sounding.** `subscribeSequencerSync`'s events subscriber iterates `Object.entries(events)` and never handles a REMOVED key, where the automation subscriber beside it does — so `removeTrack` tells the worker nothing and its notes play until the project is reloaded. Confirmed against the sync layer, filed as issue #137, and out of this issue's scope. **(4) Two defects the review found BEHIND this one, both fixed here.** Neither was reachable as a distinct symptom while every sequence played at once, and both are about a switch of active sequence being complete rather than about which tracks it selects. ⚑ **The implicit loop is DERIVED from the active sequence, so it is re-pushed wherever that derivation changes** — `pushActiveSequence` in `sequencerSync` now sends the §7.1.4 loop beside the metadata, from the `activeSequenceId`/`playbackMode` subscriber and from the `sequences` one. Without it, switching to a longer sequence left the worker looping the previous sequence's length over the new pattern, so a 4-bar sequence opened after a 2-bar one wrapped every two bars. ⚑ **A changed loop region re-bases `lastLoopPass`, because it changes what a PASS counts.** `loopPassAt` counts the CURRENT region's lengths, so widening the brace mid-playback left the counter holding a tally of the old, shorter passes while the new one counted from near zero — measured at 0 `loopWrapped` reports over 32 seconds of an 8-second loop, and with them the §7.7 per-pass overdub merge, which is the half that costs a take rather than a sound. The reference is `nextScheduleTick` rather than a clock reading, because the core has no clock (§11.3) and that cursor is already in the domain `scheduleSequence` compares against.

- **2026-09-04 (ap)** — _An insert slot's parameters are complete for the effect in it, written wherever a slot ENTERS the store (§3.4, §4.2, §5.7). Closes issue #131. Adds one exported function beside `defaultEffectParams`, one §11.4 probe seam with the two smoke steps that drive it, and one optional field on an existing offline-render options object. `defaultEffectParams` now also states the wrapper's `mix` for the five effects whose §5.7 row omits one. No schema field, DDL column, §7.1.3 message kind or store slice was removed, no §6 payload shape changed, `PRAGMA user_version` is unchanged and **no §9.2 migration was added**._ **(1) The gap.** All eight §12 phases are complete, so this is defect closure against §3.4 — "on mount, every control reflects the _current_ store value, and the store value reflects the actual node state". `createInsert` merges `defaultEffectParams` over whatever a slot holds, and `addInsert`/`replaceInsert` wrote `params: {}`, so a freshly added delay ECHOED at 350 ms and READ as the 1 ms range floor. It is not a DSP fault and nothing sounds wrong on its own: what is wrong is that every reader of the store disagrees with the node. The §14 (an) unit work is what made it legible, because before that the panel read "1" and said nothing about what the number meant. **(2) The defaults belong where a slot ENTERS the store, and there are exactly two such places.** ⚑ **The action that CREATES a slot** — `addInsert` and `replaceInsert` seed the incoming effect's own §5.7 defaults. ⚑ **The action that ADMITS one** — `setChannels` and `upsertChannel` complete an occupied slot on the way in, which is what makes a project written before this fix resolve correctly with no migration. Every route into the store passes through one of those two: §4.4 hydration calls `setChannels`, a §9.6 import and a §9.8 factory pack are read back through the same hydration, and a §6 pad's `inserts` reach the mixer through `programSync`'s `upsertChannel`. ⚑ **`createInsert` is the WRONG place and always was**, though it is where the merge lives: it is downstream of the store, so completing there leaves the store itself holding `{}` and the panel, the §4.1 gesture origin, an XYFX axis and a §7.8 automation lane all still reading the floor. Its merge nonetheless STAYS, because the §11.2 offline render helpers legitimately call it with a partial params record; after this work it is a no-op for every slot the application builds. ⚑ **The §4.3 sync layer is wrong for the same reason, and for a second one**: `mixerSync` diffs on `inserts` IDENTITY and rebuilds the whole serial chain when it changes, so a subscriber that completed the slots on the way past would allocate a new array on every pass and tear the chain down each time. ⚑ **`completeInsertParams` returns the SAME record when nothing was missing**, and `withCompleteInserts` the same array and the same strip, so a hydrate that changes nothing does not make that diff fire. ⚑ **The stored value always wins, and nothing is repaired.** The completion fills what is ABSENT. A key the effect does not declare is left alone rather than dropped: it is inert — the panel iterates `EFFECT_PARAM_RANGES` and each effect core reads its params by name — and discarding a value a project carries is not this work's business. **(3) No §9.2 migration is needed, and that is a property of WHERE the completion sits rather than a claim about what is on disk.** A project written before this fix genuinely holds `"params":{}` in its §9.3 `tracks.mixer` column and in the `master`/`returns` of `projects.payload`; it resolves because those rows reach the runtime only through `setChannels`. A migration would have had to rewrite three JSON blob columns and would still not have covered a §9.6 import or a §9.8 pack, neither of which runs migrations at all. **A project saved AFTER this fix carries the parameters explicitly**, which is the other half of issue #131's complaint — before it, what a project sounded like depended on the defaults compiled into the build rather than on anything stored. **(4) The wrapper's `mix` is part of the slot's surface, so the defaults state it.** §5.7 gives every insert `enabled` and `mix`, and the §7.8 catalogue offers `mix` on every slot — but only `delay`, `saturator` and `reverb` name one in the §5.7 table, and `createInsert` ran the other five at its own `merged.mix ?? 1`. So an `eq4`'s `insert:…:slot1.mix` read 0 (the `MIX_RANGE` floor, fully DRY) while the graph ran it fully wet, addressable by a §10.3 encoder and by a §7.8 automation lane. `defaultEffectParams` now returns `{ mix: 1, …the effect's own row }`, which changes no audio: it states what `createInsert` was already doing. `effectParams.test.ts` gates it — every parameter a slot can address must have a default, the wrapper's `mix` included. **(5) What the tests now pin.** 1842 unit tests, up from 1831, across 171 files. `insertDefaults.test.ts` drives the store: a fresh slot, a replaced one, a slot admitted through `setChannels` and through `upsertChannel`, a stored value that must not be overwritten, an EMPTY slot that must stay empty, and the identity a hydrate must not disturb. **Every regression test was proven against the unfixed code first**: 8 of its 10 assertions fail there, and the two that do not are the guards — an empty slot and the identity check pass trivially against code that completes nothing. Three existing tests pinned the defect itself and are corrected rather than added to: two asserted `params` `toEqual({})` after a replace, and `InsertPanel.test.tsx` asserted the three range floors as `aria-valuetext` ("1 ms", "200 Hz", "−6.0 dBFS"). They were written to pin the UNIT (issue #35) and they pinned the wrong VALUE with it. **(6) Measured in a real browser** (spec §13.5), not inferred: 6/6 driver checks at port 5344 and the full §11.4 smoke at 5342/5343, **67/67 steps** (up from 63), no console errors. `insertDefaultsProof` reports what the store SAYS and what the graph DOES for one delay, separately, because that is the only way the disagreement is visible: the store number is taken through the fallback every reader uses, and the graph number is an offline render of a delay built from the slot's params verbatim — the record `applyInserts` hands `createInsert`. Three slots, because a fix at the creating action alone leaves the third wrong: one just added, one after a real save and `loadProject`, and one whose `projects.payload` was rewritten to `params: {}` the way a build before this fix wrote it. All three now read 350.0 ms and echo at 350.0 ms. **Against the unfixed store all three read 1.0 ms and echoed at 350.0 ms**; the first touch of the knob then committed 600 ms and the undo returned it to **1 ms**, a value the delay had never held; and the §8.5.6 panel announced "1 ms" where it now announces "350 ms" and "35 %". **(7) Scope and honest limits.** ⚑ **A pad's §6 `inserts` never persist through the mixer.** `padStripsForProgram` publishes them into the mixer store and `mixerChannelDirtyKey` marks the owning program dirty, but `flushProgram` writes `useProgramStore`, which the mixer never writes back to — so an insert edit on a pad strip is lost on reload. Found while tracing where a slot enters the store, out of this issue's scope, not fixed, filed as issue #133. ⚑ **The §9.5 bounce applies no channel strip at all.** `renderSegments` connects every voice straight to a bare master gain, so no level, pan, send or insert is rendered, and `bounceTrack`'s "post-insert, pre-master" is not what the code does. Same discovery path, same reason for not fixing it here, filed as issue #134. ⚑ **`addInsert` enforces no slot limit.** §1.3.1 gives a strip four slots configurable to eight and `useProjectStore.globalInsertLimit` holds the number, but the action appends without consulting it — and the §7.8 address grammar bounds a slot at 8, so a ninth insert is unaddressable by automation, by Q-Link and by the panel's own knobs. Observed while writing the browser proof, which grew the master chain past that bound and lost its own gesture; the proof takes back what it leaves rather than working around it. Filed as issue #135. ⚑ **The range-floor fallbacks stay** in `InsertPanel`, in `readScalar` and in `XyfxMode`. They are what the types require of an index into a `Record<string, number>`, and after this work nothing reaches them for an occupied slot; the tests are what say so, not the absence of the `??`.

- **2026-09-04 (ao)** — _`songAdvanced { entryIndex }` addresses §7.9's position-sorted entry list, and §7.7's live erase gains the browser proof and the multi-pass rule it never had. Closes issue #130. Issue #16 was already closed by the §14 2026-07-18 work and is CONFIRMED here rather than fixed. Changes the §7.1.3 `songSequence` request shape — the first change to an existing kind rather than an addition — and bumps `SCHEDULER_PROTOCOL_VERSION` to 2. Adds one §4.2 `useTransportStore` field, one §2.6-style structural guard in `buildSongMap`, and two §11.4 probe seams with the smoke steps that drive them. No schema field, DDL column, §7.1.3 message KIND or store slice was removed, and no §6 payload shape changed._ **(1) The gap.** All eight §12 phases are complete, so this is defect closure in `src/core/sequencer/` again. Neither defect is a timing fault. `songAdvanced` reported a number §7.9 says it must not report, and nothing consumed it — so the contract was wrong in a place no test and no user could reach. Live erase was already correct and already tested, and had never been driven in a real browser at the one moment the issue is about. **(2) The playlist crosses the wire as ENTRIES, not as a repeat-expanded id list (§7.1.3, §7.9, issue #130).** `sequencerSync.flattenSong` pushed one id per repeat, so an entry with `repeats: 2` became two entries by the time the worker numbered them and `buildSongMap` stamped two different `entryIndex` values on one §9.3 `song_entries` row. §7.9 is explicit that the index is into the position-sorted ENTRY list. ⚑ **The expansion moved to the worker, which is where the map is built.** `buildSongMap` already expanded `repeats` correctly and already let an entry skipped for a missing sequence consume its index — it was simply never given the real entries. Correcting the number on the way out instead would have meant a second place that knows what a repeat is. ⚑ **The SENDER sorts and the worker takes the array index.** `position` is the §9.3 column and `songEntries` is the store's own array, so the sort belongs beside the store; the worker needs an order, not a field it would have to trust. ⚑ **`SchedulerSongEntry` carries neither `id` nor `position`.** A field the worker cannot use is one more thing that can disagree with the store. **(3) The version is bumped, and this is the first thing it has ever had to name.** `SCHEDULER_PROTOCOL_VERSION` goes 1 → 2. Every earlier protocol change added a kind or a field, which the extend-by-adding-kinds rule (§14 2026-07-17 (f), (g), (i)) leaves compatible; this one changes an existing kind's payload, so an older peer's `orderedSequenceIds` no longer parses. Without the bump the only symptom would be the §1.3 #11 guard silently dropping the message and a song that never starts — issue #71's shape exactly, which is what the version was reinstated for in §14 (am). **(4) `songAdvanced` has a consumer, so the contract is now observable.** §8.5.12's playlist marks the entry that is playing. ⚑ **The playlist RENDERS in `position` order**, which is the projection §7.9 names; it used to render `songEntries` in array order, which is the same list only because every action in that mode happens to write it sorted. §7.9 says a consumer "MUST index the same sorted projection rather than the raw `songEntries` array", and a highlight is the first thing that could have got it wrong. `removeEntry` and `moveEntry` take the sorted projection for the same reason. ⚑ **A repeat holds the mark rather than stepping off it.** Three plays of one entry are one entry, so the row stays lit for all three; the playhead INSIDE the entry is the §7.1.4 SAB's job, not this. ⚑ **The mark clears on a stop and on leaving song mode.** The worker sends nothing to clear it, and a lit row on a stopped transport claims the song is still on it — §7.9 returns the playhead to tick 0 there. ⚑ **It is ordinary React state, not a §3.3 high-frequency value.** It changes at most once per sequence pass. **(5) Issue #16 was already fixed, and what was missing was a PROOF and a RULE (§7.7).** `collectErase` was corrected to use `eventsInWindow` on 2026-07-18 and the issue was closed the same day; the handover's neighbour list had gone stale. Re-verified by reverting `collectErase` to the fold-and-min/max form: the shipped wrap test fails there, erasing `early` where it must erase `late`. ⚑ **What a sweep LONGER than one loop pass means is now recorded**, because §7.7 answers only the ordinary case and choosing silently would be inventing a rule: what goes is the UNION of the sequence ticks the window passed over, and each event goes exactly ONCE however many passes swept it. Counting instead would make the number of `erased` reports depend on the scheduler's wake rate, which is a §7.1.4 implementation detail rather than anything musical. ⚑ **Song mode's own wrap is a loop boundary too**, and a sweep armed after the first pass had no test at all. ⚑ **Song mode keeps its own erase selection.** `reportErased` is the shared half and stays shared; the selecting half genuinely differs, because a song slice is a segment window in song ticks and a sequence window is a linear window folded on the loop. One function taking either would be a flag, not a shared rule. **(6) A bound on the WORK, not on the arrangement.** `repeats` has a floor of 1 and no ceiling in §7.9, in the §9.3 column or in `songEntrySchema`, and it now reaches the worker that owns the transport — so a damaged §9.6 pack declaring a billion repeats would allocate a billion segments there. `buildSongMap` stops at 100 000 segments and warns, matching what `segmentWindow` already does for the same class of runaway (issue #95); 100 000 one-bar segments is over 55 hours. ⚑ **The §9.5 song bounce expands the playlist through `buildSongMap` too, rather than beside it.** It carried its own copy of the §7.9 expansion, on the MAIN thread, so the cap bounded the worker and left the render able to freeze the tab — and two pieces of code reading `repeats` differently is the drift the shared maths exists to prevent. §9.5 renders the same span the scheduler plays, and now says so in one place. **(7) What the tests now pin.** 1831 unit tests, up from 1815, across 170 files. `songEntryIndex.test.ts` drives the real core: a repeated entry gets one index, an entry whose sequence is missing still consumes one, the arrangement still plays every repeat, and the §7.7 capture flush still counts PLAYS while the index counts entries — the two numbers the prompt for this work warned must not be conflated. `SongMode.test.tsx` pins the consumer's half, and `sequencerSync.test.ts` pins that the playlist leaves the main thread sorted and unexpanded. **Every regression test was proven against the unfixed code first**: 3 of 4 entry-index assertions and the sync-layer assertion fail against the repeat-expanding sender, 3 of 6 playlist assertions fail against a raw-array index and the other 2 against a `stop` that does not clear, and both new erase tests fail against the code they were written to catch. **(8) Measured in a real browser** (spec §13.5), not inferred: 10/10 driver checks at ports 5342–5344 and the full §11.4 smoke, no console errors. A live-erase sweep held across the bar line of a 3840-tick loop took ticks 3600 and 0 and left 960, 1920 and 2880 standing; against the reverted `collectErase` the same sweep took 960, 1920, 2880 and 3600 and left only tick 0 — the complement, which is issue #16 in one line. A two-entry song of three plays reported entry indices [0, 1] and Song mode marked row 0, "Entry probe A", while that entry was on its SECOND play; against the repeat-expanding sender the same song reported [0, 1, 2] and marked row 1, "Entry probe B", a whole entry early. **(9) Scope and honest limits.** ⚑ **Live erase applies to the LOOKAHEAD window, so the erased region sits up to `LOOKAHEAD_MS` later than the region the user swept.** That is inherent to a lookahead scheduler — the notes under the playhead are already scheduled — and at 100 ms it is a twentieth of a bar at 120 bpm. Nothing is done about it and nothing should be. ⚑ **A held erase commits one "Delete notes" undo entry per scheduler wake.** `removeEvents` takes a `coalesceKey` and the engine's `onErased` passes none, so a sweep across a bar can spend a good part of the §4.5 100-entry history. Observed here, out of scope for either issue, not fixed. ⚑ **Sequence mode plays and erases the tracks of EVERY sequence, not just the active one.** `scheduleSequence` iterates `this.tracks` with no `sequenceId` filter where `emitSongPass` has one, so two sequences holding notes sound together and a live erase takes the held pad out of both. Found while pinning #16's "nothing else" half, verified against the real core, and filed rather than fixed: it is neither issue's scope and it is a scheduling defect rather than an erase one. ⚑ **A `repeats: 0` entry reaches the worker and is skipped there, as §7.9 says**, but `songEntrySchema` had a floor of 1 that no runtime path ever enforced — nothing `.parse()`s it, and neither the §9.3 DDL nor `mpcweb.ts` bounds the field. The floor is now §7.9's own 0 in both places; a NEGATIVE or fractional count is still refused, because that is corruption rather than an arrangement. ⚑ **`songAdvanced` still fires per ENTRY, so a one-entry song that loops announces nothing on the wrap** beyond its first pass — `lastEntryIndex` resets per pass, so it re-announces index 0 each time round, which is what §7.9's "resets its last-entry cursor" asks for. **(10) What the review caught, and what each answer is.** Three findings, all fixed, and the first two are one mistake seen twice: a value §7.9 defines was treated as one the store defines. ⚑ **The §7.1.3 guard's floor is §7.9's, not `songEntrySchema`'s.** Carrying `repeats` on the wire put it under the guard for the first time, and the guard refuses a whole MESSAGE over one bad field — so a floor of 1 dropped the ENTIRE playlist of a song §7.9 explicitly describes, leaving song mode silent with nothing logged. The old sender degraded gracefully there, contributing no ids for that entry, so this would have been a regression rather than a tightening. ⚑ **The bound belongs wherever the playlist is expanded**, and after the fix that is one place. ⚑ **A `songAdvanced` in flight when the transport stops no longer re-lights the row the stop cleared.** The worker learns about a stop on its next wake (§7.1.4), and nothing arrives afterwards to correct the mark, so a stopped transport would have shown a playing entry indefinitely. The gate is on the setter, beside the two clearing rules it belongs with.

- **2026-09-04 (an)** — _The five accessibility defects are closed: §8.2's announcer is the only live region in the shell tree (#34), every continuous control announces a human unit (#35), the shell gains a skip link, lands focus when it appears and stops promising a toolbar it cannot honour (#46), each insert bypass names its slot and its effect (#58), and the §9.7 eviction warning reaches all 12 modes (#51). Closes issues #34, #35, #46, #58 and #51. Adds one §4.2 `useUIStore` field, one optional prop on `Button`'s two siblings, one `Fader` variant and four `formatValueText` domain tokens; replaces `SOFT_CAPABILITY_LABELS` with `SOFT_CAPABILITY_NOTICES`. No schema field, DDL column, §7.1.3 message kind or store slice was removed, and no §6 payload shape changed._ **(1) The gap.** All eight §12 phases were complete and Phase 7's exit criterion included a §3.5 lens-1 sweep of every mode, so this is defect closure against that sweep rather than new work. None of the five is a rendering fault: each is a promise the accessibility tree makes and the app does not keep. **(2) One announcer, and it has two channels (§8.2, issue #34).** `LiveRegion` was mounted once and correctly, and eleven other live regions competed with it — the transport bar's unsaved-dot span, a region per toast, Q-Link Edit's two, `StoragePanel`'s two, the update prompt, the browser notice, the start gate's two, and five mode error banners carrying `role="alert"`. Competing regions produce an unpredictable order and drop announcements, which is the opposite of what a single polite queue is for. ⚑ **The announcer carries a polite AND an assertive channel, and two is the whole set.** Severity used to pick between `status` and `alert` per toast, which is exactly the competing-region defect; losing the distinction instead would have announced "Your work cannot be saved on this device" behind whatever was already being read out. Both regions are permanently mounted and {@link announce} picks between them, so severity survives as a channel rather than as a region. A third would be a competing region again. ⚑ **It is mounted by `App`, not by `AppShell`.** The overlays beside it — toasts, the update prompt, the browser notice — and the §5.1 start gate itself all announce while the gate is still up, and an announcer behind the gate would not exist to relay them. It is FIRST in `App`'s tree so its listeners are registered before any sibling's effect can announce on the very first commit. The three blocking screens (`CapabilityGate`, `AlreadyOpenScreen`, `AppErrorFallback`) render INSTEAD of `App`, so they cannot compete and keep their own regions, exactly as issue #34 says. ⚑ **`useAnnounce` is the one route a site takes, and it lives beside the announcer.** Fifteen call sites needed "announce this when it becomes non-null"; an effect written out at each of them is how a site grows a region of its own again. ⚑ **The unsaved dot is READABLE, not ANNOUNCED.** It flips on every burst of editing, so announcing each flip would talk over everything else §8.2 has to say. What the user needs said out loud — a save that succeeded or failed — the Save button and the autosave toasts already announce; the `sr-only` text stays for reading on demand. ⚑ **The notifications landmark comes and goes with the queue.** An empty labelled landmark is one more thing to step over in all 12 modes, for nothing. **(3) Human units, from the registry that already owns the ranges (§8.2, issue #35).** `formatValueText(value, '')` fell through to a bare number, so Mixer's Pan and Send knobs, every `InsertPanel` parameter knob and both XYFX axes announced things like "−0.3". ⚑ **Four of the new tokens name a DOMAIN rather than a unit**, because the number a control stores is not the number a person reads: `pan` (a −1..1 position read as "Centre" / "L 30" / "R 30"), `fraction` (a 0..1 amount read as a percentage), `faderLevel` (a §8.5.6 position read as dB through the one fader law) and `ratio` ("4.0:1"). They live in `formatValueText` rather than as a `formatValue` callback per call site because §8.2's wording is a property of the PARAMETER, not of the screen it appears on — the same send has to read the same way on a Mixer knob and on an XYFX axis. `MixerMode` and `AudioEnginePanel` accordingly drop their own `faderValueText` closures. ⚑ **`targetUnit` joins `targetRange` in the §7.8 registry, and `resolveTargetUnit` joins `resolveTargetRange` in the catalogue.** An insert parameter's unit belongs to the EFFECT in the slot exactly as its range does, so a caller holding only the address cannot answer either question; resolving the range in one place and the unit in another is one edit away from drawing a delay time in milliseconds and announcing it as a fraction. ⚑ **A dimensionless parameter reads as a bare number, and that is correct.** A filter Q has no unit, and one invented for it would be worse than none. `effectParams.test.ts` is the gate: every §5.7 parameter that is neither enumerated nor on the dimensionless list must declare a unit the formatter knows. ⚑ **The insert knob's log taper is now read from the unit rather than from a substring match on the key.** `param.toLowerCase().includes('freq') || param === 'cutoff'` and the unit table were derived side by side and only one of them was right: the delay's `tone` (200 Hz–18 kHz) and both `multibandComp` crossovers were on a linear taper. They are frequency parameters and §5.7 states cutoff as log, so this corrects them. ⚑ **`Fader` gains a horizontal variant rather than the two call sites keeping a native range.** §1.3 #10 makes every control a bespoke primitive and §3.6 forbids re-styling one at a call site, so the Grid's quantise strength and Sample Edit's transient sensitivity become the same primitive lying down. They also gain the §8.2 keyboard contract every other continuous control has, which the native elements did not share. **(4) The three keyboard defects (§8.2, issue #46).** ⚑ **The transport bar drops `role="toolbar"` for `role="group"`; the roving tabindex is deliberately NOT implemented.** The WAI-ARIA toolbar pattern promises one tab stop with Left/Right moving between the controls, and this bar holds two `slider`s and two segmented controls, each of which owns the arrow keys for its own value. A roving tabindex would have taken the arrows away from all four to satisfy a role the bar never needed — the reported defect is that an arrow silently changed the BPM of a "toolbar" the user believed they were navigating, and removing the false promise fixes that without breaking four working controls. `group` names the collection and promises no keyboard behaviour the bar does not have. ⚑ **The skip link is a BUTTON, not an `href="#…"` anchor.** §1.3 #9 rules out a router and a hash would rewrite the URL away from the §2.4 `start_url` for the rest of the session. Focusing the panel does everything the anchor would: the panel is already `tabIndex={-1}`, and Tab continues from it. Measured in a real browser, it collapses **14 tab stops to one**. ⚑ **`AppShell` takes focus when it FIRST appears**, which is the moment the §5.1 gate unmounts the button holding it. It mounts exactly once per session and only after a user gesture, so this is a gesture-driven focus move rather than an autofocus on load. **(5) Bypass, named by slot and effect (§5.7, issue #58).** Four inserts presented four buttons announced as "Enabled, toggle button, pressed", and the enclosing `<li>` had no name either. ⚑ **The verb is BYPASS and the pressed state inverts to match it.** §5.7 defines the slot's `enabled` field as true bypass via routing, so bypass is what the control does and what every desk calls the button; "Enabled" named a state rather than an action, and named it identically on all four slots. A bypass light is lit when the effect is out of circuit, so `pressed` follows the name. The store field keeps its frozen §13.6 name and the inversion lives at the one place the two meet. ⚑ **An EMPTY slot reads as empty, not as bypassed.** `createEmptyInsertSlot` carries `enabled: false`, so reading the field straight through lit all four default slots as though the user had bypassed something. The toggle is unpressed and disabled there: there is nothing to bypass. Found by driving the real DOM, not by reading the component. ⚑ **`accessibleName` joins `Knob` and `Toggle`, with `Button`'s rule.** It must BEGIN with the visible label — WCAG 2.5.3 (Label in Name) — so "Cutoff" stays the caption and "Cutoff, insert 2, Filter" is the name. **(6) The soft capabilities and the §9.7 eviction warning (§2.1, issue #51).** ⚑ **`PlatformNotices` is a strip under the transport bar, not a popover or an icon.** §9.7 asks for "a persistent dismissible warning that the browser may evict data"; it existed only inside `StoragePanel`, mounted in Q-Link Edit — mode 11 of 12 — while the always-mounted `StorageGauge` carried it as a `title`, which a keyboard cannot reach and the §1.1 tablet cannot open at all. §14 2026-07-18 (o) moved the gauge to the transport bar so storage state would be visible everywhere; this is the warning making the same move. A strip is readable without a gesture, its Dismiss is an ordinary button, and it costs no height when there is nothing to say — which on the §1.3 #15 baseline is every session where persistence was granted. ⚑ **`useUIStore.storagePersisted` single-sources the grant**, because the surface that must SHOW it is not the surface that ASKS for it. `StorageGauge` still owns the §9.7 first-run request and publishes the answer; `StoragePanel` keeps the diagnostic READING and loses its own warning, which no longer has to be found. ⚑ **Two conditions, one consequence.** A browser with no `persist()` cannot protect the data and a browser that refused the grant has not; the user loses their work the same way, so they read the same warning. Nothing is said while the request is unanswered, because "not yet asked" is not a refusal. ⚑ **`SOFT_CAPABILITY_LABELS` is DELETED, not wired.** It was a table of four short strings with no consumer; `SOFT_CAPABILITY_NOTICES` replaces it with the §2.1 copy the shell actually renders, mirroring `HARD_CAPABILITY_DETAILS`. It holds only `persistentStorage` and `wakeLock` — the two soft capabilities with no control of their own to disable. Its check:orphans allowlist entry goes with it. ⚑ **Issue #51's claim that `microphone` has zero consumers is STALE.** `LooperPanel` reads `capabilities.soft.microphone`, disables the Mic source, puts the reason in a `title` AND on the page, and `LooperPanel.test.tsx` pins all of it. It is already the compliant §2.1 treatment, as `bluetooth` is; what was dead was the labelling table, not the capability. Neither gets a shell notice, because a strip repeating in eleven modes what the owning mode already says is not an improvement. **(7) What the tests now pin.** 1815 unit tests, up from 1761, across 168 files. Three assert a NEGATIVE that no earlier test could: `AppShell`, `StoragePanel` and every `Toast` tone must contain zero elements matching `[aria-live], [role="status"], [role="alert"]`, and the running application must contain exactly two. `effectParams.test.ts` gates the label and unit tables against `EFFECT_PARAM_RANGES`, so a §5.7 parameter added to one and not the others fails rather than announcing its own store key. **Every regression test was proven against the unfixed code first**, by reverting one product behaviour at a time and re-running the tests meant to catch it: 13 mutations, 29 failing assertions, none of the 13 unnoticed. **(8) Measured in a real browser**, not inferred (spec §13.5): 27/27 driver checks at port 5342 and 59/59 smoke steps at 5343/5344, no console errors. The announcer existed while the start gate was still up; the whole running application held exactly two live regions with three toasts on screen, an error toast reaching the assertive channel and an advisory one the polite channel; the skip link was the first tab stop and reached the mode panel in one press where the bar is 14 stops deep, and focus was already on the panel when the shell appeared; the Tempo knob still stepped 120 → 121 on an arrow key, now as a slider rather than as toolbar navigation; pan read "Centre", a send "0 %" and the fader "0.0 dB" on the same strip; ten insert knobs announced ten units and none announced a bare number or a registry key; both XY axes read "0.0 dB" and "Centre"; no `input[type=range]` survived in either mode; six bypass toggles carried six distinct names and the four empty slots read as unpressed; and a refused persistence grant raised a readable, dismissible warning whose button is in the tab order, where the gauge's `title` now carries usage alone. Two probe seams are new and empty in production: `announcementProof` and `platformNoticeProof`. **(10) What the review caught, and what each answer is.** Five findings, all fixed, and the first is the same defect from the other side. ⚑ **A toast is announced by `ToastViewport`, and by NOTHING else.** Eleven call sites already called `announce(message)` right after `pushToast(message, …)` — the §8.5.4 assignment path, five Program Edit editors — so every one of them said it twice; at `warning` tone the direct call wrote the polite region while the queue wrote the assertive one, putting the identical sentence in both regions at once. That is the competing-region defect #34 exists to end, reintroduced by the fix for it. All eleven are removed and the queue is the one announcer. The two `announce` callers that raise NO toast — the Grid's automation refusal and the §8.5.7 arm-for-pad-grid message — stay, because they are announcements rather than duplicates. The regression test asserts the polite region EQUALS the message, not that it contains it: a second `announce` of the same text on the same channel appends a zero-width space, so an extra caller shows up as a trailing character rather than as a second region. ⚑ **A domain token is not a unit, so it is never suffixed onto a value that has no reading.** `pan`, `fraction` and `ratio` were handled after the §14 (ak) non-finite guard, so a NaN pan announced "— pan" and an infinite mix "∞ fraction" — readings in pans and in fractions. They now fall through to the unit-less em dash or infinity symbol. `faderLevel` stays ahead of the guard, because the §8.5.6 law answers for its own non-finite position. ⚑ **The shell notice announces what the DEVICE raises, not what is still on screen.** Built from the filtered list, dismissing one of two notices re-read the survivor; dismissing is a UI act and the device has not changed. ⚑ **A probe restores what it found.** `platformNoticeProof` left a fabricated refusal behind, so every later step of a smoke run would have read a warning the browser never gave. ⚑ **A probe seam with no smoke caller guards nothing.** Both new proofs are now driven by the §11.4 smoke — 59/59 steps, up from 55. The announcement proof deliberately raises an error and a warning toast, because the split it demonstrates is chosen by severity and §11.4 fails on either; every message it raises begins with "Probe " and the step takes its own traffic back out of the toast log. **(11) Scope and honest limits.** **A new insert still displays and announces its RANGE FLOOR while the graph runs the §5.7 defaults** — `addInsert` and `replaceInsert` write `params: {}` and `createInsert` merges `defaultEffectParams` over it, so a fresh delay sounds at 350 ms and reads as 1 ms. That is a §3.4 store-to-graph disagreement with a different cause from the missing unit, it predates this work, and the unit is what made it legible; observed and filed as issue #131, not fixed here. The `wakeLock` notice is the only §2.1 soft capability given a shell strip rather than a disabled control, because it has no control to disable; a browser meeting the §1.3 #15 baseline never sees it. Dismissal is session-scoped, exactly as `StoragePanel`'s warning was — §9.7 asks for persistent-until-dismissed, not remembered across loads. `role="toolbar"` is gone from the codebase entirely; nothing else claimed it.

- **2026-09-04 (am)** — _The three sequencer-correctness defects are closed: song mode schedules everything sequence mode does (§7.9), a note-repeat hit carries the pad that produced it (§7.3), and the §7.1.3 worker boundary enforces the contract it documents. Closes issues #94, #25 and #96, and closes #87 as already implemented by (v)/(w)/(x). Adds one field to the §7.1.3 `init` request, one engine test seam and one `NoteRenderResult` field. No schema field, DDL column, §7.1.3 message KIND or store slice was removed, and no §6 payload shape changed._ **(1) The gap.** All eight §12 phases were complete, so this is defect closure in `src/core/sequencer/` — the project's least-tested contract. None of the three is a timing fault. Song mode played bare notes and silently omitted four features and a data-safety flush; note repeat resolved a hit's owner by note number, so two tracks holding one pad collapsed into one; and the mechanisms guarding the worker boundary — a protocol version, four Zod ranges, a colon-free id assumption — were each documented and none of them enforced. **(2) Song mode does everything sequence mode does (§7.9, issue #94).** `scheduleSong` called none of `scheduleNoteRepeat`, `scheduleArpeggiator`, `scheduleSequenceAutomation` or `collectErase`, and flushed a recording only at stop. Nothing in §7.3, §7.7 or §7.8 scopes itself to sequence mode; they were simply never wired. All five now run, and none is recorded as deliberately sequence-only. ⚑ **A song's equivalent of a loop pass is one SEGMENT — one play of one sequence — and that is where a take is merged.** §7.7 merges an overdub into the track "each loop pass"; a song has no loop to wrap, so the boundary that means the same thing is the entry boundary. `lastSegmentOrdinal` counts `pass × segments + index` rather than naming the segment, so a one-entry song with `songLoopEnabled` on still flushes at every wrap instead of looking like the same segment for ever. This is the only one of the five that loses user data: before it, a crash or a tab kill mid-song lost the entire take. ⚑ **The note-repeat and arp grids are enumerated in SONG ticks, and each hit is then timed at its own segment's tempo.** A grid re-phased per segment would stutter at every entry boundary; the song tick is monotonic within a pass, so the grid stays locked to the song's bar line. Swing and the note's duration convert at `segment.bpm`, exactly as a written note's do (§7.9), and the emitted `tick` is the segment's sequence tick — the domain a written note carries and the only one a capture may be recorded in. ⚑ **A sequence-scope automation lane belongs to the sequence PLAYING, not to `activeSequenceId`.** `effectivePoints` filtered sequence lanes against the transport's active sequence, which in song mode is whichever the user last selected — so every other sequence's automation was silent for the whole song. It now takes the sequence as an argument: the active one in sequence mode, the segment's own in song mode. ⚑ **A track lane is sampled at the ABSOLUTE SONG TICK, a sequence lane at the segment's sequence tick.** §7.8 says a sequence lane "loops with the pattern" and a track lane "spans the song arrangement", and song mode is the first place those two sentences differ: a track lane sampled per pattern would restart at every entry. Sequence mode is deliberately unchanged and still samples both at the wrapped sequence tick, because there the pattern IS the arrangement and there is nothing to span. **(3) A capture in song mode was landing on a tick its own pattern could never reach.** Found while wiring the per-pass flush and fixed with it, because flushing an unusable take more often is not the fix #94 asks for. `pushLiveNote` recorded at `positionTickAt(when)`, which in song mode is an absolute song tick (§7.9) — so a pad played during the third bar of a song was written to `midi_events.tick_start` at ~4800 on a 3840-tick pattern, past its end, where it never sounded again. `captureTickAt` folds the song position onto the segment's sequence tick; `positionTickAt` still answers where the PLAYHEAD is, which in song mode is genuinely a song tick, so the two are separate functions rather than one with a flag. Sequence mode already returned a sequence tick and is unchanged. **(4) A note-repeat hit carries the pad that produced it (§7.3, issue #25).** `heldNotes` is correctly keyed `${trackId}:${note}`, so one pad held on two tracks is two entries — but the scheduler recovered a hit's owner with `held.find(h => h.note === hit.note)`, which matches on the note number alone and returned the first entry for both. Two note-ons fired on the first track, the second sounded nothing, and while recording both captured notes were written into the first, silently losing the second track's part. §1.3.1 maps a pad index straight to a note number, so layering two drum programs and holding one pad on both is ordinary MPC practice rather than a corner case. ⚑ **The fix removes the lookup rather than correcting it.** `NoteRepeatHit` is generic over the held entry and carries it as `pad`, so there is no second chance to resolve an owner wrongly — the arpeggiator already got this right by grouping on `trackId` first. `OpenNote` gained its own `trackId` and `note` for the same reason: `closeOpenNotes` used to split its map key back apart, and a rule about ids restated at a call site is exactly the shape of this defect. **(5) `SCHEDULER_PROTOCOL_VERSION` is attached and checked, not deleted (§7.1.3, issue #96).** The issue offered either. §7.1.3 titles the protocol "typed, **versioned**" and §13.1 requires each handover to record the worker protocol versions, so deleting it would drop a constraint the spec states; it now rides the `init` handshake and the worker compares it with its own copy. ⚑ **A mismatch is REPORTED and initialisation continues.** The two halves are emitted by one Vite build and loaded from one page, so skew is not reachable today, and the §1.3 #11 Zod guards already drop precisely the messages a mismatched build cannot read — refusing to start would turn a partial disagreement into a dead transport. What the check buys is a NAME for that silence: §11.4 fails the smoke on any console error, so a real skew fails the gate instead of presenting as a sequencer that quietly does nothing, which is issue #71's shape exactly. Adding a request or response kind still does not bump it (the extend-by-adding-kinds rule, (f)/(g)/(i)). **(6) The guard is no looser than the store it mirrors.** `tempo.bpm`, `sequenceMeta.tempo` and `projectBpm` were bare `z.number()` and `swing.amount` unbounded; all four now take the §4.2 ranges the store already clamps to. Measured with the guard bypassed: a negative bpm makes `beatSeconds` negative and the §7.7 click loop emits its whole 4096-click structural guard on every wake, for ever; a swing amount of 100 shifts a 1/16 fully onto the next grid line. ⚑ **Ids are guaranteed colon-free, and `messages.ts` is the one place that is checked.** `SchedulerCore` keys three maps `${trackId}:${note}` and its lanes `${scope}:${ownerId}:${targetPath}`, then counts separators to split them — sound only while an id carries none, which §1.3.1 guarantees by making every id a `crypto.randomUUID()`, the §9.8 generator derives, and `remapSnapshot` refuses a §9.6 import for breaking. Stating it at the boundary §1.3 #11 already designates as the worker's validation contract is what lets the splitters be arithmetic. A §7.8 `targetPath` is deliberately exempt: colons are its grammar. **(7) One implementation of the automation-emission rule, and one of the lane-key split.** `automationRampForWindow` was reachable only from its own two tests while production carried a hand-rolled copy inside `scheduleSequenceAutomation`. It survives and both modes now call it; the copy is gone. ⚑ **Its value tick is passed separately from its two times**, because the two are in different domains wherever the transport wraps — sequence mode times from the monotonic linear tick and samples at the wrapped sequence tick, song mode times from absolute song seconds and samples at the segment's sequence tick. A single `tickToSeconds` callback could express neither without the caller re-deriving the other half, which is how the second implementation came to exist. `parseAutomationLaneKey` joins `automationLaneKey` in `schemas/sequence.ts` and replaces FOUR open-coded splits: two in `SchedulerCore`, one in `sequencerSync.ts` — that fourth one also cast the scope to its union without checking it, so a malformed key would have reached the worker claiming a scope the guard then rejected, dropping the lane in silence. **(8) What the tests now pin.** `schedulerWire.test.ts` drives every `SchedulerClient` sender through `parseSchedulerRequest` **and** `applySchedulerRequest`, asserting what reached the core; its sender table is compared against `SchedulerClient.prototype`, so a sender added without a case fails rather than going untested. `applyEventsDiff`'s delete-before-upsert ordering, the §7.7 count-in gate, and `automationValueAt`'s behaviour on duplicate ticks, a single-point lane and unsorted input are each asserted rather than assumed. Every regression test was run against the unfixed code first: 10 of 11 song-parity assertions and all 3 note-repeat ownership assertions failed there. 1757 unit tests, up from 1712. **(9) Issue #87 was already closed by the code.** §14 (v) made the declick exact for a retuned voice, (w) integrated the whole detune contour — pitch envelope, keygroup glide and pitch-routed LFO — and (x) settled the bend by summing it onto its own node. All three §6 modulators and both live-retune paths are covered by 13 tests in `detuneSchedule.test.ts` and 6 in `voicePool.test.ts`. What was missing was a real-browser measurement rather than an implementation, and `declickContourProof` supplies it. **(10) Measured in a real browser**, not inferred: 21/21 driver checks at port 5350, no console errors. Song mode emitted 114 automation ramps climbing 0.05 → 0.44 across a three-entry song where the unfixed build emitted none; live erase removed all four notes it swept; a take played into the middle entry was merged into the track **while the transport was still rolling**, at tick 961 of a 3840-tick pattern where the unfixed build wrote 4800 at the stop; one pad held on two tracks produced 6 repeats on each at their own velocities (100 and 60) where the unfixed build produced 12 on the first at one velocity; the arpeggiator ran in song mode, keeping [60, 64] on one track and [72, 76] on the other; the handshake carried version 1, the guard refused all four out-of-contract messages and still admitted a versionless handshake so it could be reported; and a voice swept an octave down sounded 0.4552 s against the flat voice's 0.2995 s, ending on a magnitude of 0.00102 rather than a step. **(11) What the review caught, and what each answer is.** Three findings, all fixed, and two of the three are one rule seen twice: a captured note has TWO domains and folding them independently loses the relationship between them. ⚑ **A capture measures its LENGTH on a monotonic cursor and anchors its START to the pattern.** Folding the note-on and the note-off separately put them in different segments across a song entry boundary — and behind each other across a sequence-mode loop wrap — so the subtraction went negative and `captureAt`'s `Math.max(1, …)` floor collapsed a held note to one tick. `capturePositionAt` returns both domains at once and `captureHeldNote` combines them, which also corrects the loop-wrap case in SEQUENCE mode, where it was latent long before this work: a note held across a wrap was recorded a tick long. No cap is imposed on the resulting duration, because §7.7 states none and choosing one would be inventing a rule. ⚑ **A position past the last segment is clamped INTO it.** §7.9 closes open notes on the stop path, and the playhead is then at `songTotalTicks` — past every segment — so the no-segment fallback wrote the very out-of-pattern tick this work exists to prevent, at the one moment a take is most likely to still be held. ⚑ **`init.protocolVersion` is OPTIONAL, and it is the only optional field in the request protocol.** Making it required had the §1.3 #11 guard drop the handshake of the very build the version is meant to name: the SAB was never installed, the transport was dead, `applySchedulerRequest` never reached its own report, and §11.4 saw no console error — precisely the silent failure the check was added against. The schema uses `.catch(undefined)` rather than a plain optional so a version this build cannot even parse still reaches the reporting branch, and the message reads "sent no version". **(12) Scope and honest limits.** `AudioEngine.watchScheduledEvents` is new: the scheduled batch is the worker's only observable output and nothing downstream distinguishes a note from a click from a ramp, so a browser proof of what song mode SCHEDULES has to read the batch itself. It is empty in production and costs one `size` read per event. `NoteRenderResult.finalMagnitude` is additive. **`songAdvanced { entryIndex }` still indexes the FLATTENED playlist, not §7.9's position-sorted entry list**: `sequencerSync.flattenSong` expands `repeats` before the ids reach the worker, so an entry with `repeats: 2` consumes two indices where §7.9 says it should consume one. Observed while here, not fixed, and not in any of the three issues' scope — filed as issue #130.

- **2026-09-03 (al)** — _The five reachability and performance defects are closed: the §9.5 song bounce, stem bounce and resample-to-pad reach a real surface (#104), export streams instead of holding the whole archive (#99), the §4.1 transient channel leaves React entirely (#27), Grid scroll and zoom leave it too (#28), and the destructive Program Edit actions are gated by what the user cannot see (#54). Closes issues #104, #99, #27, #28 and #54. Adds two §2.5 primitives, one optional prop on `Knob`/`Fader` and on `ValueReadout`, and replaces the `pack` worker message with a four-message pack session. No schema field, DDL column, §7.1.3 message kind or store slice was removed, and no §6 payload shape changed._ **(1) The gap.** None of the five was a DSP or timing fault. Three of the §9.5 bounces wrote a correct WAV that nothing could retrieve, export held a project's audio twice over, two continuous-gesture paths re-rendered every consumer of a store map per frame in breach of §3.3, and four destructive edits fired on first tap with nothing on screen saying they were undoable. **(2) A bounce the user cannot reach has not been produced (§9.5, issue #104).** `/projects/{id}/bounces/` is OPFS: no part of the UI browses it, no file manager opens it, and a `.mpcweb` export packs the project rather than its bounces. A control that reports success and produces nothing retrievable is worse than the dead control §3.4 forbids, because a dead one does not lie. ⚑ **The stem bounce lives on the Mixer's track strip**, because a stem IS that strip — post-insert, pre-master (§8.5.6) — and `bounceTrack` had no caller anywhere in the repository. ⚑ **Resample-to-pad opens the assignment chooser on the sample it just made.** §9.5 names the feature resample-to-**pad**; stopping at a library row would leave its last step for the user to find. It is the only one of the four whose result is not downloaded, because the sample library is a surface the Browser already lists. `downloadBlob` moved out of `BrowserPanel` into `core/platform/download.ts`, which is why the paths that never had it never had it. **(3) One packer, and it streams (§9.6, issue #99).** `packMpcweb` took every sample's bytes as one argument and `zipSync` held the finished archive besides, so exporting peaked at roughly twice the project's audio — on the tablet §11.5 targets, the one way a large project could fail to export at all. `createMpcwebPacker` replaces it outright: **there is no second synchronous packer**, because two pieces of code writing one format is the drift the §9.6 layout constants exist to prevent, and the §9.8 factory generator drives the same packer with a pinned `exportedAt`. ⚑ **`project.json` and `manifest.json` are written LAST.** A caller reading samples one at a time only learns which it could not read as it goes, and the §9.6 completeness check compares the rows against the audio — so the snapshot cannot be serialised until the last sample is in. Zip entry order is not part of the §9.6 layout and `unpackMpcweb` reads by name. ⚑ **Each sample's buffer is TRANSFERRED, and each output chunk becomes a `Blob` at once.** Without the transfer the structured clone copies the audio and the peak is unchanged; without the per-chunk `Blob` the finished archive is back on the JS heap. The peak on either side of the worker is one sample plus one chunk. ⚑ **Only one zip entry is open at a time**, because fflate's `Zip` buffers a later entry's output until the earlier one completes. A pack is now a session (`packBegin`/`packSample`/`packEnd`/`packAbort`) keyed separately from the request id, since `packChunk` is output rather than a reply. **What this does NOT do:** the import side still materialises each entry before validating it — bounded by the §2.6 budget since issue #26, but not streamed into the database. **(4) The §4.1 transient channel never touches React (§3.3, issue #27).** `setTransient` was an ordinary `set()`, which replaces the containing map's identity, so `MixerMode`, `MutingMode`, `XyfxMode` and `QLinkEditMode` re-rendered on every pointer sample and every rAF-aligned CC frame — about sixty a second while an encoder turns — and Q-Link Edit rebuilt thousands of `<option>` elements inside each, in the very mode the user is in while turning that encoder. `src/store/transientChannel.ts` carries the value to the §4.3 sync layer instead. ⚑ **The overlay is the live value and the store is the committed one.** A relative §10.3 encoder steps from where the parameter is NOW, so something has to hold that once the store stops moving; that is why this is a channel with memory rather than an event bus. ⚑ **A commit PUBLISHES before it settles.** The committed value need not be where the gesture left off — XYFX's release-return commits where the axes RESTED — and a store diff cannot cover it, because the store never moved and so sees no change. ⚑ **An insert-parameter drag no longer rebuilds the insert chain.** `mixerSync` diffs on `inserts` identity and rebuilds the whole serial chain when it changes, so dragging one delay's feedback used to tear down and recreate every effect on the strip per pointer sample; `bridge.applyParam` ramps the one `AudioParam`. That was an audio defect hiding inside a render defect. ⚑ **§10.3's "UI reacts concurrently" is kept by ref, not by re-render.** `Knob` and `Fader` take an optional `livePath` and paint an out-of-band move the same way they paint their own drag, which is what §3.3 permits. **(5) Grid scroll and zoom leave React too (§3.3, §8.4, issue #28).** The canvas already mirrored the viewport into a ref and painted from it, so the React round trip bought nothing at all. The viewport is now a store the rAF loop and the pointer handlers read directly. ⚑ **The zoom readout is painted by ref; only the three buttons' `disabled` flags go through React**, and those are discrete — they cross a clamp a handful of times per gesture rather than every frame. ⚑ **There is deliberately no re-sync effect after each render.** The rendered value never changes, so React never writes the text node; an effect there would be code no test could fail against, which reads as necessary and is not. ⚑ **The store compares fields rather than identity before notifying**, because a wheel held against the start of the timeline produces a stream of updates that change nothing. **(6) What counts as destructive enough to confirm (§8.1, issue #54).** The test is what the user can SEE going, not whether the action is undoable. ⚑ **Confirm an action that destroys a container whose contents are not on screen** — Delete program, Clear pad — and count what is at stake rather than only warning. ⚑ **Do not confirm an action whose whole subject is the row it sits on** — one layer, one route, one insert, one song entry. A dialog on every row makes editing unusable on a touch device, and §4.5 lists each as undoable; the finding was that nothing SAID so, which a toast naming Undo fixes. ⚑ **Do not confirm a §8.5.4 sample edit at all**: §8.5.4 has them render a new OPFS file and swap a pointer, so the original survives until the §8.5.7 purge. ⚑ **The §8.5.7 purge and the §8.1 hard reset keep the opposite wording**, because those genuinely are outside the undo stack. The rule lives on `ConfirmDialog`, in one place. **(7) The long operations say they are working.** A `<label>` has no `disabled`, which is why every import control was hand-rolled from the button classes and none was gated on `busy` while every sibling button was — so a user who saw nothing happen tapped again and imported the project twice. `FilePickerButton` puts the `disabled` on the input, where it refuses the click however the label is pressed, and `buttonChassis` is exported so it shares the §3.6 chassis rather than copying it.
