The Looper holds every transport control from the moment a take stops until its bytes are written, which is the window its Record button used to be live in. **(8) What the review caught, and what each answer is.** Four findings, all fixed, and all the same shift seen from four sides: a gesture reaches the graph instead of the store, so anything that used to read the moving store for free now has to say where it reads from. ⚑ **A voice built mid-gesture reads the LIVE program, not the committed one.** `bridge.applyParam` acts on nodes that already exist, so it moves a sounding voice and cannot move one not yet built — and `programParamChange` maps neither `amp.attack` nor `amp.release`, which are two of the four §10.3 pad-mode encoder defaults, so a whole turn of either changed nothing at all until the idle commit. The engine's two live paths resolve through `programWithLiveGestures`, which returns the same program untouched when nothing is in flight, because it runs per note. The §9.5 bounce deliberately does not call it: a render is of committed state, and a knob held mid-gesture is not part of the arrangement. ⚑ **A commit SETTLES before it does anything else.** Replacing a slot's effect, or deleting a pad, inside the 250 ms idle window makes the address stop resolving — and an early return left the overlay answering for it forever, so the next relative encoder turn stepped from an abandoned value and a later undo reverted to one the parameter never held. The mixer needed `canonicalPathOf`, which derives the overlay key WITHOUT the strip that `parseMixerPath` requires for an insert range. ⚑ **Main's always-mounted Master fader took `livePath` too.** It is the one fader on screen in every mode, a project-mode Q-Link encoder is bound to it by default, and it sat still through a turn while the audio and the meter moved. ⚑ **A control says what IT is doing, not that something is happening.** One shared `busy` flag gated every Browser control correctly and labelled them all wrongly: a bounce relabelled the import pickers “Importing…” and marked them `aria-disabled`, which is the opposite of the missing-progress problem §54 is about. `FilePickerButton` separates `busy` (what this control is doing, and so its label) from `disabled` (every other reason it cannot start). **(9) Scope and honest limits.** A §10.3 encoder turn moves an on-screen control only where the call site passes `livePath` — the five Mixer controls and Main's master fader do; Program Edit's plain number fields do not, and update at the commit as they always did. `audioBridge.resyncAll` reads committed state, so an engine restart mid-gesture would land the graph on the pre-gesture value; nothing in the app restarts the engine mid-gesture. Unpacking is unchanged. No §5.7 effect parameter, DDL column or §9.3 payload field was added or removed.

- **2026-09-03 (ak)** — _The five correctness-hardening defects are closed: mod-matrix sums are clamped where they reach a parameter (§6), the §4.3 audio-param helpers and the §5.6 kernel wrappers guard against non-finite and out-of-range values, `decodeWav` refuses a malformed `fmt ` chunk (§9.4), the three §7.1 silent-failure modes report or disarm, and a mid-playback tempo change applies from the change onward (§7.2). Closes issues #76, #97, #66, #95 and #74. No schema field, DDL, message protocol or store slice was removed, and no §6 payload shape changed._ **(1) The gap.** Each of the five let a value nothing validates reach the audio path, or let a failure report success. None loses stored data; each makes the instrument lie about what it is doing. **(2) The mod matrix (§6, issue #76).** `modMatrix.ts` documents its result as "left un-clamped so the voice builder can clamp against each target's own range", and the voice builder honoured that for the filter only. §6 caps the matrix at 32 routes and each `amount` at ±1 while forbidding nothing about all 32 landing on one target, so a program that passes Zod validation — an imported `.mpcweb` pack included (§9.6) — detuned 32 octaves and peaked at 33× gain. `clampModSum` bounds each summed target to the ±1 full scale that target declares, and `oscillatorDepthScale` scales the LFO depths in proportion, because those sum in the audio graph where nothing can clamp them. The voice builder then clamps the resulting detune and amp peak at the parameter, against limits derived from the §6 ranges rather than written down. **(3) The guards (§4.3, §5.6, issue #97).** ⚑ **The ramp helpers REFUSE a non-finite value; they do not substitute one.** A NaN written to an `AudioParam` poisons that param and everything downstream of it for the rest of the session, with no error and no way back but a reload — the worst failure those helpers can cause, so it is the one they will not perform; substituting a number would keep the graph sounding at a value nobody asked for and nothing recorded. They deliberately do NOT clamp to a range: a helper that takes any param cannot know its range, and the §4.1 store actions, the §6 schemas and the §7.8 registry each already own that. ⚑ **The kernel wrappers CLAMP the value and REFUSE the structure.** An out-of-range `f64` is not an error inside an AssemblyScript kernel, it is a coefficient, so a NaN release time becomes a NaN state variable and the kernel outputs NaN for life; a numeric §5.7 parameter is therefore coerced into range (a non-finite one collapsing to the range floor, the quietest end of every parameter these wrappers expose), while a bad block size, sample rate or band index throws, because there is no defensible value to substitute for one of those. ⚑ **Those §5.7 bounds are declared beside each wrapper rather than imported from `effectParams.ts`,** because the wrappers load inside the DSP-effect worklet (§5.6.2) and importing the effect table there would drag `@/core/project/schemas` — and Zod behind it — into the render thread; `kernelRanges.test.ts` is the §13.6 gate that stops the two copies drifting. ⚑ **The fader law clamps and reads a non-finite level as true silence,** which is recoverable by moving the fader where a poisoned param is not. The §7.8 registry now bounds the insert slot against the §1.3.1 insert limit and the pad index against the 128-pad range, and rejects a non-canonical index that would not round-trip — §7.8 makes `isAutomatable` the gate on what may be STORED, so an address that parses but can never resolve let a §9.3 `automation_points` row persist against a parameter that does not exist. `equalSlices` refuses more slices than the sample has frames; `enforceMinSpacing` floors its spacing at one frame, which does not tighten §7.5 (markers are frame indices) but does drop the duplicates a spacing of 0 used to keep; `RingBuffer` refuses a fractional slot count; `SampleCache` evicts by promise identity rather than by path; and `formatValueText` no longer puts the literal "NaN" into `aria-valuetext`, which a screen reader announces verbatim. **(4) The WAV decoder (§9.4, issue #66).** The missing-`fmt ` case named in the issue was already closed by the #17 work; what remained was a `fmt ` chunk that is present but wrong. A chunk DECLARING fewer than 16 bytes still had 16 read out of it, so `bitsPerSample` came from the next chunk's header; a second `fmt ` chunk silently won; and a `blockAlign` contradicting the channels and bit depth beside it was ignored. All three now refuse, and the format fields have no defaults at all — they are undefined until a chunk supplies them, so there is nothing for a malformed header to be decoded against. ⚑ **`encodeWav` refuses ragged channels rather than zero-padding them.** The frame count comes from channel 0, so a shorter channel was silently padded — the encoder inventing audio the caller never had, which is the wrong way to report a caller bug. **(5) The sequencer (§7.1, issue #95).** ⚑ **A truncated lookahead window DEFERS; it neither drops nor throws.** `segmentWindow` now returns how far it reached, and `scheduleSequence` advances its cursor only that far, so the remainder is scheduled on the next wake — dropping it silently was the defect, and throwing would stop playback outright over a window that is merely large. It warns, so the anomaly is visible. ⚑ **A torn seqlock read HOLDS the last good reading and reports itself stale.** It used to return tick 0 as a real position, which read as the playhead snapping to bar 1 mid-playback; the holding is done inside `PlayheadReader` rather than at each call site, so no consumer has to remember to. ⚑ **A stop disarms live erase.** Erase is armed by holding a pad over Erase (§7.7) and a stop released neither for the worker, so the next playback silently deleted notes the user never asked to erase. Clearing it costs a user still holding Erase through a stop one re-press; not clearing it cost their notes. **(6) The tempo change (§7.2, issue #74).** ⚑ **The tempo anchor is now SEPARATE from the count-in boundary.** `SchedulerCore` computed position as `originTick + secondsToTicks(now − contentStartContext, bpm)` and `setTempo` moved only `bpm`, so changing tempo re-read the entire elapsed history at the new tempo: halving it halved the computed position and the playhead jumped backwards with no time having passed, by an amount that grew with how long the transport had been running. A tempo change now re-anchors — `anchorContext`, `originTick` and `anchorSongSeconds` move to the moment of the change, carrying the tick and song seconds measured at the OLD tempo — so the elapsed part is immutable and the new tempo governs only what follows. `contentStartContext` stays where content genuinely began, because that is what the §7.7 count-in gate compares a live note's timestamp against; folding the two into one field would have made a §10.2 latency-compensated note, timestamped just before the change, look as though it had arrived during a count-in. ⚑ **The re-anchor happens at the next `tick(now)`, not inside `setTempo`.** The core has no clock of its own (§11.3 — the worker file is a thin message shell), and re-anchoring needs a trustworthy context time; the wait is at most `SCHEDULER_INTERVAL_MS`, and nothing reads a position in between. ⚑ **`nextScheduleTick` is deliberately left where it is,** since it marks how far the lookahead has already posted; on a slow-down it sits ahead of the playhead and the scheduler simply schedules nothing until the clock reaches it, which is why no event is dropped or double-scheduled across the change. The §7.7 click grid is re-phased the same way: the last click emitted keeps its place and the grid is rebuilt from there at the new spacing, skipping any click the new spacing would put in the past. **(7) What the review caught, and what each answer is.** Five findings, all fixed. ⚑ **`positionTickAt` no longer short-circuits on a zero elapsed.** It returned `originTick` raw, which was safe only while that tick could not move; a re-anchored one is an arbitrary point part-way through a loop pass, so the wake applying a tempo change published an unwrapped linear tick into the playhead SAB. ⚑ **The click re-phase rebuilds the grid around the NEXT unemitted click, not the last emitted one, and skips no index.** Anchoring on the last emitted click and then advancing past any beat the new spacing put in the past dropped an index, and the §5.9 accent follows the index — so every later accent came off the bar line. The next click now takes the earliest of where the old tempo was going to put it and one new beat from here, never earlier than now: the beat the change interrupts finishes at the tempo it started under, and a large speed-up does not wait out the old, longer beat. ⚑ **The accent phase is captured once at the play gesture** (`clickBeatPhase`) rather than re-derived from `originTick`, because the anchor moves and the bar the grid is on does not. ⚑ **`scheduleClicks` will not emit a click more than one beat stale, but still consumes its index.** A past `when` reaches Web Audio as "play now", so a wake after a backgrounded tab used to dump every elapsed beat as one burst; the tolerance is a beat rather than zero because a click a few milliseconds late is still the beat the user expects, and dropping it would silence the metronome. ⚑ **A non-finite kernel parameter is REFUSED, not floored.** The first cut sent it to the range minimum and called that "the least destructive end", which is false: −60 dB is a compressor's hardest threshold, 500 Hz collapses a crossover's mid band and −6 dBFS is the limiter's tightest ceiling. `kernelParam` now returns null and the setter skips the write, so the kernel keeps what it had; `kernelParamOr` covers the parameters that genuinely have a neutral — a stretch rate of 1, a pitch shift of 0, the documented detection defaults — where refusing would leave a warp voice with no stream prepared, which is silence. ⚑ **A non-finite voice tune is no detune.** `clamp` maps NaN to its minimum, which here was four octaves flat — the opposite of `clampModSum`'s rule that a value nobody can interpret contributes nothing. A non-finite amp gain still lands on silence, because that one has no neutral to fall back to and silence is recoverable where a poisoned gain param is not. **(8) Scope.** No §5.7 effect parameter, §7.1.3 message kind, DDL column or §9.3 payload field was added or removed. `EFFECT_PARAM_RANGES` is unchanged; the kernel copies of three of its entries are new, gated by test. `segmentWindow` and `PlayheadReading` changed shape — both are internal to `src/core/sequencer/`.

- **2026-09-03 (aj)** — _The five data-integrity defects are closed: a project switch refuses rather than discards (§4.4), tempo and swing persist to the §9.3 sequence row, `.mpcweb` import fails loudly on a damaged pack and export recovers per sample (§9.6), the storage layer gains one policy for a recoverable failure (§9.2), and zip decompression is bounded while unpacking (§9.6, §9.7). Closes issues #103, #93, #99, #98 and #26. Adds five §2.6 constants, one §4.2 `useProjectStore` field and one store→store subscriber. No schema field, DDL, message protocol or store slice was removed._ **(1) A failed flush now refuses the switch (§4.4, issue #103).** `flushNow()` never rejects, by design, so a failed pre-switch write was reported only through its return value — and `loadProject` discarded that value, then called `teardownAutosave()`, whose `dispose()` cleared the batch `runFlush` had just re-queued for retry. The toast named the loss while hydration removed the project the user would have exported from, so the fix for #19 had converted a silent data loss into a narrated one rather than a prevented one. ⚑ **The decision is REFUSE, not confirm.** Issue #103 leaned towards a confirmation modal; the refusal is stronger and needs no new UI, and the escape hatch it costs is already covered three ways: the message tells the user to export, the queue is left standing so a later `saveNow()` (or a retry of the switch) can still write, and a permanently-unflushable batch is dropped by the queue's own §14 issue-#72 rule, so the second attempt at the switch proceeds. `newProject` and `installUnpackedAsNewProject` flush FIRST rather than inheriting the refusal from the `loadProject` at their end, so a refused switch leaves neither an empty project nor a half-installed archive behind. ⚑ **`closeActiveProject` forces instead**, because §8.1's Safe Mode exists to get the user out of a shell that is already failing and its own offer is to export — refusing there would trap them in the state they are escaping. `AutosaveQueue.unsavedKeys` is new and is what lets the refusal SAY what it is refusing over: `pendingKeys` alone cannot answer, since a permanent failure clears the queue by design. `describeDirtyKeys` turns that set into "2 sequences and 1 track", because a raw `sequence:<uuid>` tells a user nothing they can act on. **(2) Tempo and swing reach the row that owns them (§9.3, §7.9, issue #93).** `setBpm`/`setSwing` wrote only `useTransportStore`, which §4.2 documents as the derived mirror of the active sequence — so `sequences.tempo` and `swing_amount` never moved, autosave was never armed, the unsaved dot never lit, and the next hydrate put the edit back. `src/store/tempo.ts` is now the one place a tempo or swing edit enters the model, called by the §8.1 transport bar and the §10.3 Q-Link runtime alike, with the §4.1 transient/commit split: the gesture moves the mirror, the commit writes the row. ⚑ **A tempo edit is UNDOABLE.** §4.5 exempts "transport actions" from undo, but tempo is arrangement data on a sequence row, and §4.5 lists sequence changes as undoable — so it commits through `updateSequence` like every other persisted control. ⚑ **With no active sequence the edit lands on `projects.bpm_default`**, which §9.3 already defines as what a NULL `sequences.tempo` means; `useProjectStore.bpmDefault` is the new §4.2 field holding it, and `flushProject` now writes that column, which nothing ever did. Swing has no project-level default in §9.3, so with no sequence it moves the mirror and no more. `src/store/derive/transportMirror.ts` re-derives the mirror from `(active sequence, project default)` and is deliberately NOT part of `syncLayer/` (§4.3 is store→graph; this is store→store). One subscriber rather than a line in `updateSequence` and another in `setActiveSequenceId`: two places drift, and it also fixes two adjacent bugs — an undo restored the row while the bar kept showing the undone tempo, and switching sequence played the new one at the old one's tempo. **(3) A damaged `.mpcweb` fails loudly, and export recovers per sample (§9.6, issue #99).** Three layers each assumed a different one was checking, so an archive missing half its `samples/*.wav` imported with no error and was discovered by pressing play. `unpackMpcweb` now reconciles the archive's audio against the sample rows `project.json` declares and refuses the import when any row has no bytes behind it — before a row or a byte is written, which is what makes §9.6's "a failure mid-way leaves no partial project" mean something for a damaged file. Audio no row references is still accepted: it wastes space in the archive and nothing else. `project.json`'s own `version` is now gated (`PROJECT_SNAPSHOT_VERSION`), where only the manifest's `formatVersion` used to be — a snapshot claiming `version: 999` parsed and imported against schemas that had never seen it. `remapSnapshot` asserts the UUID shape its substring rewrite has always relied on and only documented in a comment; §1.3.1 makes every id a `crypto.randomUUID()` and the §9.8 generator derives UUID-shaped ids, so nothing real is excluded. ⚑ **Export omits what it cannot read rather than refusing outright**, and says so. One `Promise.all` over the sample reads meant a single missing OPFS file made a project impossible to export at all — the worst possible moment to be strict, since export is what a user reaches for when a project is already misbehaving. An unreadable sample is dropped from the archive AND from the snapshot's sample rows, so the file stays internally consistent and re-imports cleanly rather than tripping the completeness check above; the pads that played it import with a dangling reference and no sound, which is honest, because the audio genuinely is gone. A warning toast names how many were left out, since a quietly smaller export is the silent loss this issue is about. **`dumpSnapshot` now copies in the global-library samples the project's programs reference**, which §9.6 requires ("global-library refs are copied in") and `samples WHERE project_id = ?` never found: §9.8 factory audio de-duplicates into `/global_library/` with `project_id = NULL`, so a project built from factory content exported with no sample rows and no audio, and imported silent. Membership is a substring hit on the raw program payload, exactly as `findUnusedSamples` decides the §8.5.7 purge. **What is NOT fixed:** export still holds every sample in memory at once — `zipSync` materialises the whole archive, and streaming it would be a redesign of `pack.worker.ts` and `packClient.ts` rather than a fix. #99 stays open for that alone. **(4) One policy for a recoverable storage failure (§9.2, §9.7, issue #98).** `mapResultCode` collapsed extended SQLite result codes for the CONSTRAINT family only, so `SQLITE_BUSY_RECOVERY` (261) and `SQLITE_LOCKED_SHAREDCACHE` (262) fell through to `SQLITE_ERROR` and `isRetryable` said false for exactly the lock contention §9.2's write queue exists to survive; the low byte is now masked generally, with the foreign-key code checked first because it is the one extended value this app distinguishes. `DbError.isRetryable` had no caller at all before this. `src/core/storage/retry.ts` is the policy: transient contention (SQLite BUSY/LOCKED, OPFS `NoModificationAllowedError`) is retried up to `STORAGE_RETRY_ATTEMPTS` with a doubling backoff, everything else propagates unchanged — the ORIGINAL typed error, never a "gave up" wrapper the UI cannot branch on. It wraps the three data operations on `WorkerDatabaseDriver` and `writeFileAtomic`, all of which are safe to repeat: a statement that reported BUSY never ran, a transaction that failed rolled back, and the atomic write removes its temp file before rejecting. `init`, `diagnostics`, `exportBinary` and `close` are deliberately outside it. `fileExists`, `deleteFile` and `deleteDirectory` caught everything, so an I/O or permissions failure reported "absent" or "deleted" and a caller could not tell "not there" from "could not tell" — only `NotFoundError` is now an answer. ⚑ **`writeFileAtomic`'s fallback is documented as best-effort rather than made atomic**, because without `FileSystemFileHandle.move()` there is no atomic replacement to reach for: the honest position is that §9.7's "never corrupt a half-written file" holds wherever `move()` does, which is every browser meeting the §1.3 #15 baseline. `opfs.test.ts` covered only path building; the handle operations are now driven by an in-memory OPFS fake (`src/test/fakes/opfs.ts`) that proves the policy, with the real path still proven by the §11.4 smoke per §13.5. **(5) Bounded zip decompression (§9.6, §9.7, issue #26).** `unzipSync` materialises everything before returning, so there was no moment at which a limit could apply and a ~1 MB `.mpcweb` of compressible data could reach gigabytes inside `pack.worker.ts` before §9.7's headroom check ever ran. Unpacking now streams through fflate's `Unzip`, checking the entry count, the per-entry size and the running total **while inflating** — inside `ondata`, where a throw unwinds straight out of `push` because the decoders are synchronous, so the remaining bytes are never inflated. That is a bound on memory rather than a report of how much was used. An entry the §9.6 layout does not describe is never started, so it costs nothing at all. `MPCWEB_MAX_ENTRIES`, `MPCWEB_MAX_ENTRY_BYTES` and `MPCWEB_MAX_TOTAL_BYTES` join the §2.6 registry, and `unpackMpcweb` takes an optional budget so the limits are provable at a size a unit test can build. A malformed archive now reports as unreadable rather than surfacing fflate's own "invalid zip data". Path traversal was re-checked and remains non-exploitable for the reason recorded in #26. **(6) Three consequences the review caught, and their rules.** ⚑ **Refusing over unsaved work and TEARING THE QUEUE DOWN are separate steps.** `newProject` and `installUnpackedAsNewProject` refuse before they write anything, so a refusal leaves neither an empty project nor a half-installed archive; but tearing the queue down at that point meant any later failure — a rejected `create`, a §9.7 headroom refusal, a rolled-back restore — left the STILL-OPEN project with `markDirty` a no-op, the unsaved dot dark and every later edit lost on reload. That is a worse loss than the one being prevented, so `assertOutgoingWorkIsSaved` refuses and the teardown stays in `loadProject`, where the project actually changes. ⚑ **An export that exceeds the import budget is written, and warned about.** Import enforces the §2.6 budget and export enforced nothing, so BangerBox could write an archive it would then refuse to open — while one of those refusals tells the user to ask for a fresh export. It warns rather than refusing for the same reason export is per-sample recoverable at all: the file may be the only copy of their work, so producing it and saying what is wrong beats producing nothing. ⚑ **The transport mirror writes back only what the ROWS changed.** Re-deriving both values on every source change looked equivalent and was not: a §4.1 transient gesture holds the mirror ahead of its row on purpose, and an unrelated edit to the same row — another §10.3 encoder committing swing during a tempo turn — snapped it back mid-gesture, so the turn's own commit then persisted a tempo nobody chose. A genuine tempo change still overrides a gesture, which is right: the mirror is a mirror, and the row moved.

- **2026-09-02 (ai)** — _Five §5/§7 settings that were reachable to set and changed nothing audible are applied at playback: §6 `warp` and `reverse`, §6 `LfoConfig.sync`/`phaseOffset`/`retrigger`, the §7.5 non-destructive groove path, §7.9's end of song, and the §5.7 synced delay division. Closes issues #84, #107, #71, #101 and #70. Adds one §5.7 effect parameter, two §7.1.3 message kinds, three §9.3 payload fields, one §4.2 store field and a streaming entry point on an existing §5.6.4 kernel. No schema field, DDL, message kind or store slice was removed._ **(1) The gap.** Each of the five had a live control, persisted correctly, survived a reload and reached no audio code — the same §3.4 breach §14 (ah) and (ag) closed elsewhere. Two of them were dead on the wire rather than merely unwired: every `groove` message was dropped by the worker's Zod guard, and song mode ran past its end with nothing scheduled. **(2) `warp` means pitch decoupled from duration, not tempo-following ⚑.** §5.7.9 gives the granular engine a `rate` and a `pitch` and says a warp pad is a real-time worklet source; §6 gives the pad a `warp` boolean and no base tempo. Tempo-following would need a field the schema does not have, and inventing one is a §13.6 halt. So warp keeps `rate` at 1 and routes the pad's own detune — tune, pitch envelope, pitch LFO, §7.8 pad-detune automation — through the granular source instead of through `playbackRate`. An octave-up warp pad therefore sounds an octave up and lasts exactly as long as the sample, where the same pad without warp lasts half as long. That is the audible difference, and it is the one §5.5 describes when it says plain `playbackRate` is for where coupled repitch is _intended_. **(3) The warp source is a real worklet, not a pre-render.** §5.5 makes granular time-stretch an `AudioWorkletProcessor` in so many words, so `granularStretch` gains a streaming entry point (`createStream` / `prepareStream` / `streamBlock`) beside its offline `render`, and `granular-source` hosts one kernel per region channel. Three consequences are recorded rather than hidden. The streaming path does **not** run the WSOLA correlation search ⚑ — it is roughly 3 M multiply-adds per grain at 48 kHz, far outside a render quantum's budget (§5.5), and at warp's own default the Hann grains sum to unity so there is nothing to correlate; `render` keeps the search, where latency is free. The region is **copied per voice** into the kernel's linear memory, because `processorOptions` are structured-cloned and a real-time source has to read at a rate it does not write at; a warp pad on a long sample is the case to watch. And start and stop are frame-accurate against `currentFrame` rather than quantum-accurate, because a scheduled note arrives up to `LOOKAHEAD_MS` early (§7.1.4) and a source that produced from the moment its node existed would already be a tenth of a second into its own region. **(4) A warp voice's declick does not move on a retune ⚑, and that is correct.** §5.4 derives the end of a voice by integrating its detune contour, because on a buffer source detune IS the playback rate (issue #87). On the warp source it is not, so the end is the source's own length and `rescheduleDeclick` returns early. The pool tells the two apart through `VoiceSource.pitchCoupled` rather than by asking what kind of node it holds. **(5) A synced LFO's rate is resolved at note-on ⚑.** #107 asked how a synced modulator should answer a mid-playback tempo change, and for an LFO the answer is: the next note. Re-rating a running `OscillatorNode` cannot preserve its phase, and the §5.4 declick has already integrated the pitch-LFO rate curve to place a fade — so a mid-note re-rate would move a fade that is scheduled. §10.5 keeps tempo automation out of v1, so the only way to reach the stale case is editing BPM with a note still sounding, and it lasts one note. The **delay** answers the same question differently, and deliberately: it retunes immediately ⚑, over the standard dezipper. A `DelayNode` has no repeat boundary to wait for — its line is read continuously — and waiting would leave it out of time for up to a whole division. **(6) `phaseOffset` is baked into the waveform.** An `OscillatorNode` starts at phase zero and has no phase parameter, so a shifted LFO gets a `PeriodicWave` whose harmonics are each rotated by `k × φ`. Whether the browser renders that basis the way the maths assumes is the one thing here that no type declaration could settle (§2.7), so a §11.2 render asserts it in a real browser: a sine rotated a quarter turn starts at +1.0000. A zero offset keeps the native type. **(7) `retrigger: false` is a property of the PAD, not of the voice ⚑.** A free-running LFO whose oscillator died with its note would restart at phase zero on the next note, which is `retrigger: true` by another name. The pool therefore keeps one oscillator per (pad, LFO index) for its own lifetime, rebuilding it only when the §6 config changes. The cost is an oscillator per pad-LFO that outlives its notes; a voice's link into it is cut by hand on teardown, since disconnecting a gain releases its outputs and not the oscillator still feeding it. **(8) The `as` cast was the defect, not the missing member.** `schedulerRequestSchema` was annotated `z.ZodType<SchedulerRequest>` **and** cast to it; the cast is what let a 15-member Zod union satisfy a 16-member type, so `groove` compiled with no guard and every groove message was dropped in silence (#71). Both casts are gone, so the compiler now enforces exhaustiveness. The worker's own `switch` moved into `applySchedulerRequest`, leaving `scheduler.worker.ts` the thin shell §11.3 describes — and giving tests the real dispatch to drive, which is what the new wire round-trip test does for every sender. The §4.3 subscriber's test stub is now a Proxy that grows its own spies, because a hand-listed set of mocks is the same drift trap one layer up. **(9) A groove has to survive a reload, or it was never applied ⚑.** The wire fix alone left the Grid's picker permanently empty: nothing called `setGrooveTemplate`, and neither the templates nor the track assignments persisted. Sample Edit now offers **save** beside **bake** — §7.5's two ways of spending a groove, from one extraction — and both the templates and the assignments join the §9.3 project payload, which §9.3 already named as their home. A template is keyed by its SOURCE SAMPLE'S NAME rather than a fresh UUID ⚑: `grooveTemplates` carries no name field, the picker shows the key, and re-extracting from one sample should replace its template rather than add one no user could tell from the last. **(10) Song mode's cursor is in absolute song SECONDS ⚑.** A looping song has no monotonic tick — pass 2's tick 0 is the same number as pass 1's — so a tick cursor cannot express a lookahead window that straddles a wrap without either double-scheduling or dropping the seam. Absolute seconds are monotonic; the window is split per pass and each pass converts its own slice. **The end is reached by the playhead, not by the lookahead ⚑**: stopping when the window first ran past `songTotalTicks` would cut the last `LOOKAHEAD_MS` off every song. Open notes close and the take flushes in the same result that reports `songEnded`, which is §7.9's stated ordering and the last chance to persist a recording. A zero-length map stops whatever `songLoopEnabled` says, because there is nothing to loop over. **(11) The delay line is sized for the worst case, once.** A `DelayNode` fixes its length at construction, and the longest §5.7 synced division at the slowest §4.2 tempo is nine seconds — so that is what every delay allocates, rather than truncating a synced delay at 40 bpm into something that silently mistimes. `sync` is index-encoded like the filter's type and the saturator's curve, so the whole surface stays automatable through `InsertSlotState.params` (§7.8); `EFFECT_PARAM_CHOICES` gives all three of those a named select in the insert editor instead of a knob reading `7`. **(12) What the gates could not have caught.** `check:orphans` reasons about exports, not about an interface field that is populated and never read, and `check:stubs` reads a tag, not a sentence. Nothing in `verify` can see a persisted schema field that no code consumes. The browser smoke gains five steps that can: each of the five settings is now asserted by an offline render through the real path (§11.2), including the `createPeriodicWave` basis check. 50/50 smoke steps, 1461 unit tests. **(13) Five defects the review found, and what each cost.** _A re-saved template did not reach the worker._ The §4.3 subscriber watched only `trackGrooveIds`, so re-extracting a groove under the same key — the very workflow item (9) settles on — left the scheduler shaping notes with the template it was handed first. It now watches the templates too. _The song ended at the first pass's end._ Turning `songLoopEnabled` off during pass two stopped the transport at once, because the test compared `now` against where pass one finished. It now compares against the end of the pass the SCHEDULER has emitted for ⚑ — which near a wrap is the pass the lookahead has already committed to, since those notes are going to be heard and cutting them off would be the worse answer. _A mode switch replayed everything since the start._ The two cursors are independent, and song mode left the sequence one at the origin; §8.5.12's picker is live while the transport rolls, so the first sequence-mode wake scheduled every tick since then in one burst, with a loop wrap and a capture flush per pass swept. Each mode now carries the other's cursor forward, so a switch resumes at the elapsed position. _A synced LFO ran at the wrong tempo in song mode._ Live dispatch stamped the transport's `bpm` while the §9.5 bounce used the segment's, so the same note rendered at a different LFO rate than it played at. `ScheduledEvent` gains an optional `bpm` (an additive §7.1.3 field): the scheduler stamps the tempo it placed the note against, which in song mode is the segment's own (§7.9), and a live audition carries none and falls back to the transport. _Replacing a free-running LFO cut the notes still sounding through it._ A §6 config change disconnected the old oscillator outright, so a sustaining voice's filter or pitch snapped to its unmodulated value mid-note and a pitch route's scheduled declick was left in the wrong place. A replaced oscillator is now RETIRED rather than stopped ⚑ — no new voice takes it, the voices already on it keep it, and it is released by reference count when the last of them ends.

- **2026-09-02 (ah)** — _The §7.8 automation-authoring seam is closed: the Grid lane is editable, the Grid gains §8.5.2 marquee select, and live gestures write points while recording. Adds two §2.6 constants, one playhead-SAB flag and one store-action return type. No schema field, DDL, message protocol or store slice was removed._ **(1) The gap.** `setAutomationLane` had exactly one caller in the repository and it was `persist.test.ts`. Automation could be scheduled (§7.1.4), imported (§9.6), persisted (§9.3) and drawn on the Grid canvas, and no user could create a single point by any route — the same shape of breach as §14 (ag)'s, against §7.8 and §3.4. §7.8's "Record automation" sentence and §8.5.10's "recordable as automation when recording" had no implementation at all, and §8.5.2's marquee select was absent. **(2) The lane is scaled to the registry, not to its own contents ⚑.** While it was a read-out, the lane scaled to `automationBounds(points)` — the span the lane happened to hold. That is wrong for an editor twice over: an empty lane has no span to scale against, and a pixel would mean a different value every time a point moved. The lane now takes its bounds from `targetRange` for the target's registered address, falling back to the data's own span only for a lane whose address the registry does not know. Such a lane is READ-ONLY and says so: a project saved against an older registry must still load and be readable (§4.4 hydration bypasses the gate deliberately), but §7.8 forbids adding points to it. **(3) Scope is an explicit control, not an inference ⚑.** §7.8 makes track scope override sequence scope for the same target, so editing the wrong one is silent. The Grid therefore names the scope beside the parameter picker rather than deriving it from whichever lane exists, and states the precedence on screen when both scopes hold the same target. Any track's lane wins, not only the selected track's, because `SchedulerCore.effectivePoints` resolves by target path across every lane. **(4) The recording tap sits at the transient/commit channel ⚑.** §7.8 names "Q-Link/knob movements" and §8.5.10 names XYFX. All three, and every on-screen knob and fader, reach the graph through `useMixerStore.setTransient`/`commit` and `useProgramStore.setPadParamTransient`/`commitPadParam` (§4.1), so `src/store/automationRecord.ts` taps those four rather than each mode. A mode cannot forget to opt in, and no mode carries capture logic of its own. **(5) Captured points are SEQUENCE-scoped ⚑, which §7.8 does not fix.** A pass captures a performance over the pattern being recorded into, and it must loop with that pattern the way the notes captured beside it do. Track scope spans the arrangement and is the Grid's to author deliberately. A pass with no active sequence records nothing rather than inventing an owner. **(6) Thinning, and what a pass overwrites ⚑.** `AUTOMATION_MIN_TICK_SPACING` (a 1/32 note, 120 ticks) and `AUTOMATION_VALUE_EPSILON` (0.005) join the §2.6 registry. The epsilon is a FRACTION of the target's registered range, not an absolute: one absolute epsilon is deaf across a 20–20 000 Hz cutoff and meaningless across a −1..1 pan. Both gates must be cleared, because spacing alone still records a stationary encoder once per window and epsilon alone records every jitter of a noisy analogue pot (§10.4). A recorded point supersedes the span the pass has just swept, so a second pass over a region rewrites it instead of interleaving two takes into a staircase; a loop wrap opens a fresh sweep rather than writing backwards. **(7) The playhead SAB gains a capturing flag.** §7.7 gates note capture on the count-in having passed, and `isRecording` is true throughout that count-in while the playhead is parked at the start tick. Without a separate flag every move made during the count-in would land as a point at the start tick. `PlayheadWriter.write` takes it as an optional fourth argument, so nothing that wrote three is broken. **(8) A move stalls rather than deletes ⚑.** Dragging a point onto a tick another point holds is refused for that sample. Two points at one tick make a lane whose value depends on array order, and deleting the obstacle would let one drag across a dense lane wipe every point it passed — an accidental erase is a worse outcome than a drag that will not go where it is pushed. Drawing, which is a deliberate placement, does replace a point at its own tick. **(9) Marquee select is per region.** A select-tool drag takes notes from the part of the rectangle over the note grid and automation points from the part over the lane, so a drag spanning both selects both and a drag confined to one selects only what it covers. The rectangle lives in a ref and is painted by the canvas's existing rAF loop; no pointer sample passes through React state (§3.3, §8.4). Notes are taken on INTERSECTION, not containment — a note longer than the swept band is plainly one the user dragged across. **(10) One catalogue feeds both pickers.** `src/core/audio/params/catalogue.ts` answers "what can this thing automate" from the §7.8 registry's own builders, and both the Grid's lane picker and the XYFX axis pickers read it — XYFX's smaller private copy is gone, along with the hand-rolled address split in its value reader. Transport addresses are deliberately absent: `audioBridge.applyAutomation` ignores them, and §10.5 records BPM automation as roadmap (issue #65). **(11) The §7.8 gates live in one place.** `setAutomationLane` is the only route a point can enter the model by, so it is where the registry check and the Zod validation sit, and it stamps every point with the lane it is written into rather than trusting the point to name it. It returns an `AutomationEditResult` carrying a finished refusal sentence, the same shape and for the same reason as §14 (ag)'s `AssignResult`. An emptied lane is deleted rather than stored empty, because the §4.3 sync layer keys its diffs off the lane map. **(12) A gesture with no move phase still records ⚑.** `useContinuousControl` sends an arrow-key step and a double-click reset straight to `onCommit`, with no transient before it (§4.5 "keyboard steps are discrete"). §7.8 asks for knob movements, and those are movements: a capture pass that opens and closes in a single sample writes its one point rather than finding nothing to close. **(13) The value epsilon is scaled by a range the CALLER supplies ⚑.** The recorder cannot resolve an insert parameter's bounds itself — they belong to the effect in the slot (§5.7), and reaching for it would make the recorder import the store that imports the recorder. The store that owns the parameter has already resolved that range to clamp the value, so it passes it down. `resolveTargetRange` in the §7.8 param catalogue is the single lookup both the Grid's lane bounds and the XYFX axis ranges use, for the same reason. **(14) The `'end'` capture runs BEFORE the parameter's own `commit()`.** A pass writes its points under one coalesce key; an unkeyed commit closes that run, so capturing afterwards stranded the closing point as an undo entry of its own. Sealing is likewise part of `resetAutomationRecording`, not an afterthought: leaving the key open folded the next take on the same lane into the previous take's entry. **Coalescing is per open key, not per lane**, so a gesture driving two parameters at once — an XYFX drag — interleaves two lanes' writes and splits each lane's run. That is a limitation of the shared §4.5 command stack, not of capture, and it is recorded rather than worked around: the entry count still does not scale with the number of points captured.

- **2026-09-02 (ag)** — _Sample assignment implemented end to end; closes issue #37. Adds one §6-adjacent constant and records the assignment policies §6 and §8.5.7 leave open. No schema field, DDL, message protocol or store slice was removed, and no §6 payload shape changed._ **(1) The gap.** `dragDropPayload` was written in one place and read nowhere; no store action could put a `sampleId` on a velocity layer or a keygroup zone; `LayersEditor` offered only Remove; and `chopSampleToNewSamples` wrote slice rows and assigned none of them. A user could therefore import audio and never make a pad sound, which breached §8.5.7, §8.5.5, §8.5.4 and §3.4 at once. **(2) The seam.** `useProgramStore` gains `addPadLayer`, `setLayerSample`, `addKeygroupZone` and `setZoneSample`. Each records exactly one undo entry (§4.5 lists pad assignment as undoable) and returns a result whose refusal carries a finished sentence, because the store is the only layer that knows which §6 rule refused. **(3) Band and zone placement, which §6 does not fix ⚑.** §6 says velocity layers may not overlap but not how a new layer gets its band. Adding a layer re-splits the whole 0..127 axis across the pad's layers, so overlap and uncovered velocity are impossible by construction rather than by rejection; a rule enforced by rejection would leave a user with four layers unable to add a fifth sound without hand-editing three spinners first. `removePadLayer` is part of the same rule and not a plain filter: the layer below the removed one grows to cover the band it held, since a pad silent above half velocity is the same defect as a pad with no layers at all. Every other boundary stays where the user put it. Zones follow a DIFFERENT rule, because §6 permits zones to overlap and `selectKeygroupZone` returns the first covering zone — so a zone added at 0..127 behind an existing one would never sound (§3.4). A new zone therefore takes the widest uncovered stretch of keyboard, and halves the widest existing zone only when no gap is left; nothing else moves, so a hand-mapped multisample survives an assignment. `removeKeygroupZone` leaves the freed keys uncovered, which §6 permits and the next assignment reclaims. **(4) `maxLayers` ⚑.** §6 writes `0..maxLayers (default cap 4, configurable to 8)` and no field carries it. `DEFAULT_MAX_VELOCITY_LAYERS = 4` joins the existing hard cap `MAX_VELOCITY_LAYERS = 8` in `schemas/ranges.ts`; the action takes an optional override, so raising it later needs no schema change. The soft default is applied by the action and the hard cap by Zod, so a payload carrying five layers still loads. **(5) `dragDropPayload` is an armed selection, not a live drag ⚑.** §8.5.7 asks for "drag-to-pad assignment (`dragDropPayload`)", and on this shell a pointer drag from the Browser to a pad cannot complete: Browser and Program Edit are two of the twelve §8.5 modes and only one is on screen, so no moment exists at which a sample row and a pad are both visible to a pointer. The payload therefore behaves as a selection the user arms in one mode and spends in another: the target chooser's "Choose on the pad grid…" arms it and switches to Program Edit, where the pad grid and the keygroup zone panel show what is armed and a press assigns it. Real `dragover`/`drop` handlers sit on both for a drag that begins and ends inside one mode. `DragDropPayload` gains `rootNote` (an additive §4.2 field): a zone needs the sample's §9.3 unity pitch, or an armed kick rooted at 36 would land rooted at middle C and sound two octaves out. **(6) The direct picker.** Two dialogs, because the two directions need different things. `SamplePicker` (target known, sample missing) opens from Program Edit's "Add sample…" and from each layer's and zone's "Change sample"; it lists BOTH §9.1 roots rather than following `useBrowserStore.currentPath`, since a pad plays whichever root holds its sample and §9.8 factory audio installs globally. `AssignTargetDialog` (sample known, target missing) opens from the Browser row, which is what makes that row's old "ready to assign — open Program Edit and choose a pad" toast land somewhere real. Both are operable by keyboard alone, which §8.5.7's pointer drag never was on a touch device. **(7) Chop placement (§8.5.4).** A "Slices to" selector chooses pads (the default), a new drum program, or the library only. Slices REPLACE a pad's layers rather than stacking under them — a chop divides one sound into many, so slice 3 velocity-layered beneath slice 2 would be nonsense — and the whole placement is one undo entry. **(8) Sample paths honour both §9.1 roots (bug fix).** A §6 payload records only a `sampleId`, and §9.3 lets that row live under `/projects/<id>/samples/` or in `/global_library/`. Live playback and the §9.5 bounce both reconstructed the project path alone, so any global sample on a pad — every §9.8 factory kit sound, once de-duplicated — was silent with no error, the read having failed and the note been skipped. `sampleCandidatePaths` returns both, project first, and both call sites try them in order. **(9) Empty-state copy.** The §8.5.5 empty states now say what the state costs the user ("No layers yet, so this pad makes no sound.") and name the step out of it, which `EmptyState`'s own header recorded as blocked on this issue.

- **2026-07-19 (af)** — _`check:stubs` gains a phase-prose gate, and the stale phase-deferral comments it now forbids are corrected; closes issue #12. No runtime code changed._ §13.6 made `check:stubs` fail from Phase 7 onward so no deferred wiring could survive the polish phase, but it only ever read the `// STUB(phase-N): reason` tag. Deferrals written as prose in a file header — "the polished 12-mode shell is Phase 7", "the query-backed Browser UI … land in Phase 6" — were invisible to it, so the gate reported "no open stubs" against outstanding work for as long as those headers stood. Rewording a tag into a sentence was a way around the one mechanical check §13.6 has. **The gate.** Every phase reference in `src` is now a failure unless it matches `scripts/check-stubs.allowlist.json`, which admits only history: §12 phase-plan citations, exit-criterion citations, `added … in Phase N` provenance notes, `before Phase N` notes about data an older build persisted, Phase 0 scaffolding names, and the frozen `phase3-demo` identifiers. The allowlist follows `check:lang`'s precedent — patterns with a stated reason, stripped from the line before the scan. **The comments.** Every remaining forward-looking phase reference was checked against what the code actually does and rewritten to describe the present. Most named work that had since shipped (`.mpcweb` export/import described as "stubbed here" when it runs in the pack worker; the Browser, Q-Link runtime, BLE transport, arpeggiator, worklet reverb and program-scope automation addresses all live). Three were genuinely outstanding and now link their issue instead of a phase: Looper mic capture (#2), non-destructive schedule-time groove (#71, also the reason `projectPayload` still has no groove-template field), and the §8.5.5 graphical editors (#56). The four inert `audioBridge` hooks are relabelled with the module that actually owns each concern rather than a phase they were once expected to gain behaviour in. Two user-visible strings in the Program Edit empty states said "assign a sample from the Browser (Phase 6)" and no longer leak a phase number at the user.
- **2026-07-19 (ae)** — _§11.2 no longer claims golden buffers live in `src/test/fixtures/`; closes issue #10. Spec text only — no test was added, removed or retimed, and no source file changed._ **(1) The disagreement.** §11.2 named a directory that has never existed: `src/test/` holds `drivers/`, `fakes/`, `mocks/`, `setup.ts` and `wasmKernel.ts`, and nothing in `src`, `scripts` or `e2e` reads a golden buffer from disk. Every DSP assertion builds its own input — `granularStretchKernel.test.ts` and `lookaheadLimiterKernel.test.ts` synthesise sine tones, `transientDetectKernel.test.ts` builds a drum-like signal and asserts each detected onset lands within one analysis hop of its true position, and the effect renders driven through `offlineTest.ts` by the §11.4 smoke feed a generated tone through each insert. The §11.1 "golden bytes" for the WAV encoder are likewise inline byte literals in `wav.test.ts`, not a file. **(2) The implementation was right, so the spec moved.** Synthesised inputs are deterministic, self-documenting and reviewable in a diff; a committed buffer is opaque, drifts with sample rate and encoder, and cannot be checked by reading the test. Adding fixtures to satisfy the sentence would have made the suite worse to serve the prose. Per §0 the deviation is recorded here rather than left implicit, and per §1.3 the choice between amending the spec and adding the directory was put to the human developer, who ratified the amendment. **(3) The door is not shut.** §11.2 still permits a stored golden where a synthesised signal genuinely cannot express the assertion — it now requires that such a buffer carry its provenance, which is the property a bare `fixtures/` directory would not have guaranteed. **(4) Nothing to verify at runtime.** The change is one sentence of specification; the suite it describes was already passing and is unaltered.
- **2026-07-19 (ad)** — _The three real-browser smokes now run in CI; closes issue #15. Supersedes the "not in CI" notes recorded in (m) item (5), (n) item (4) and the `build` job's comment. CI configuration only — no schema, DDL, store, sequencer, audio or UI behaviour changed, and no smoke script's assertions were touched._ **(1) What was unproven.** CI ran type-check, lint, `verify`, `format:check`, the unit suite and a production build — none of which touch a browser. §13.5 is explicit that the `:memory:` driver and mocked bridges are for logic and never prove the real OPFS/SAB/worklet path, and both regressions the smokes were written to catch (the module-scope `SharedArrayBuffer` throw of (m) item (4), and the one-shot `sessionStorage` bootstrap flag of (n) item (1)(a)) were invisible to a green unit suite and a successful build. **(2) A `smoke` job on `windows-latest`, and why that runner.** All three smokes drive the system-installed Edge (`channel: 'msedge'`, §1.3 #13) rather than downloading a browser binary, and only the Windows image ships Edge preinstalled. Choosing the runner to fit the locked decision leaves §1.3 #13 intact and changes no script — the alternative, relaxing the channel to a bundled Chromium for CI only, would have amended a locked decision to save runner minutes that a public repository does not pay for. **(3) The job's shape.** `build:wasm` and `build:factory` run explicitly before `test:e2e`, because `browser-smoke.mjs` spawns vite's dev and build directly and so never passes through the `npm run build` wrapper that would otherwise produce the DSP kernels and the factory pack index. `test:pages` and `test:gate` then run against a rebuild carrying `BANGERBOX_BASE=/BangerBox/`, since both assert the Pages path where isolation has to come from the service worker. **(4) The job is not fast, and that is accepted.** It builds the app twice and drives a real browser through three suites; it is the only thing in CI that proves the shipped path works at all. **(5) One latent flake, found by the first CI run and fixed here.** `gate-smoke.mjs` B3 asked `navigator.serviceWorker.getRegistration()` once, immediately after `goto`, and so reported "never registered" on a runner slower than the dev machine — while the very next assertion in the same scenario, isolation achieved with storage blocked, proved the worker had in fact registered. It now polls with a deadline. The guard is not weakened: a bootstrap that genuinely never calls `register()` still fails, just after the timeout. This is the point of the exercise — a second machine surfaced a timing assumption baked into a suite that had only ever run on one.
- **2026-07-18 (ac)** — _The Looper gains its §8.5.8 controls: bar-locked length, overdub, clear, live input meter and progress ring; closes issue #3. Supersedes the "Phase 7 polish" deferral recorded in (h) item (5) for everything but the mic source._ **(1) Bar-locked length.** Length is chosen in bars (1/2/4/8) and resolved to a frame count at record time through `barsToTicks` + `ticksToSeconds` against the active sequence's time signature and the effective transport tempo, so it is correct across tempo and metre changes; resolving at record time rather than on selection means a tempo change between takes sizes the next take instead of mis-sizing the one already running. The take is written at exactly the target length — silence-padded when the worklet's 128-frame quanta come up short, truncated when they overrun — which is what keeps successive overdubs phase-aligned. **(2) A "Free" length is kept** alongside the bar-locked ones, preserving the (h) open-ended capture; §8.5.8 does not forbid it and resampling an arbitrary passage is the Looper's other real use. **(3) Capture is decomposed** into stop / save / clear, replacing the single "Stop & save" button: `Looper` now holds the take across operations rather than saving and discarding it in one step, so overdub has something to layer onto and save can be repeated. Overdub sums onto the held take and may exceed unity; the WAV encoder's existing clamp is the only limiter, matching how the rest of the audio path treats sums. An empty capture pass leaves a held take alone rather than destroying it. **(4) Meter and ring add no loops.** The live meter is `MeterCanvas` on the existing `master` slot through the shared §5.8 meter loop; the progress ring rides the capture drain rAF the Looper already runs and writes `strokeDashoffset` straight to the SVG, so neither passes through React state (§3.3) nor adds a second rAF (§8.4). **(5) Mic source remains deferred** pending the `getUserMedia` capability gate (§2.1) — it is the one §8.5.8 bullet this does not deliver. 1021 unit tests, type-check, lint, `npm run verify` and `format:check` pass; verified in Edge against a production preview build (ring advances and the capture stops itself on the bar line, meter tracks the master bus, overdub/save/clear enablement follows the held take).
- **2026-07-18 (ab)** — _The Prettier backlog is cleared and `format:check` joins CI; closes issue #11. Supersedes the deferral recorded in (m) item (6). Formatting only — no schema, DDL, store, sequencer, audio or UI behaviour changed._ **(1) The backlog.** (m) omitted `format:check` from `.github/workflows/tests.yml` so CI would not be born red against 25 pre-existing Prettier-dirty files. Unpoliced, that count had drifted to 35 by the time this was picked up — the issue's own headline figure of 23 was already stale, which is the drift argument making its own case. `npm run format` fixes all 35. **(2) A rule conflict the reformat exposed, and the reason this is not purely mechanical.** `eslint.config.js` re-asserts `curly: ['error', 'multi-line']` after `eslint-config-prettier` (which disables it defensively), so a braceless `if` body is legal on one line but not once wrapped. Six long braceless bodies in `browser-smoke.mjs`, `inserts/effects.ts` and `GridCanvas.tsx` sat under the print width only because they were unformatted; Prettier wrapped them and `curly` then rejected all six. The two tools genuinely disagree here, and running one without the other leaves the tree failing the other — so the fix is braces (satisfying both), not a rule relaxation. **This is why the two halves had to land together:** enabling the step without the reformat, or the reformat without re-running lint, breaks CI either way. **(3) The step.** `format:check` now runs in the `quality` job after `verify`, so drift cannot silently return. 1014 unit tests, type-check, lint, `npm run verify` and the production build all pass.

- **2026-07-18 (aa)** — _Design-system consolidation (GitHub issue #49): the button and field-label chassis promoted into primitives. Extends the §2.5 primitive inventory; no §8 constraint changed._ **(1) `Button` and `FieldLabel` added to `/ui/primitives/`.** §3.6 requires reusable visual behaviour to live in a primitive variant and never be re-styled at call sites, but neither chassis had a primitive, so both were hand-rolled everywhere and had drifted into user-visible inconsistency: disabled buttons faded to four different opacities (`opacity-30/40/50/60`) depending on the mode, five different hover affordances were in use, and of the sites carrying a transition only five used the token easing `ease-bb-snap` while many identical buttons carried none and snapped instantly. `Button` settles this at one disabled treatment (`opacity-40`, matching `Toggle` and `SegmentControl`), one transition on every button, and one hover affordance per variant. **(2) Variants name intent, not appearance** — `default` (standard raised chassis), `accent` (the confirming action in a group), `quiet` (a secondary action that recedes until hovered), `danger` (destructive) — so a call site chooses meaning and the look stays consistent app-wide. Sizes are `sm`/`md`/`lg`, with `lg` reserved for the page-level call to action on a blocking screen (capability gate, storage self-test) and the only size that steps up to `rounded-bb-md`; the radius travels with the size rather than sitting in the shared chassis, so no two class sources can contend for it. **(3) `accessibleName` for row-scoped buttons.** A visible label of "Audition" or "Remove" is ambiguous when a screen reader lists the buttons out of context, so `accessibleName` sets `aria-label` while keeping the visible text. It is documented as required to _begin with_ the visible label, per WCAG 2.5.3 (Label in Name), so speech-input users can activate the button by saying what they see — this preserves the contextual names the migrated call sites already had rather than flattening them. **(4) `FieldLabel` collapses three drifted copies into one:** `gap-1.5` (Grid, Browser, Insert), `gap-2` (Pad Perform, Q-Link, XYFX), and a third _inside the primitives_ — `ValueReadout` rendered the same caption with `tracking-wide`. Settled at `gap-1.5`, tracked; `ValueReadout` now renders `FieldLabel` itself, so the primitive-internal variant is gone. It defaults to a wrapping `<label>` (most call sites contain their control, which associates the two without an id) with `as="span"` for captions naming something that is not a form control. **(5) Neither primitive accepts a `className`,** so §3.6 holds by construction rather than by convention. **(6) Deliberately not migrated:** two-state buttons carrying `aria-pressed` with state-computed colour (the pad-bank selector, note-select, favourites) belong to `Toggle`/`SegmentControl`, not `Button`; pad grids, `GridCanvas` cells, mode-rail items and canvas overlays carry load-bearing layout the chassis cannot express; and the `<label>`s wrapping hidden file inputs only look like buttons — converting them would break the file picker. `AppErrorFallback` is left independent of the primitive tree on purpose, being the error boundary's safe-mode fallback. **(7) `WaveformEditor`'s private `EditorButton` deleted** — it was the shared chassis re-declared inside a primitive, and was still at `opacity-50` with no transition; a browser audit of computed styles across seven modes confirmed it was the last surviving drift. **No audio, sequencer, storage or schema behaviour changed;** every accessible name and `data-testid` is preserved, and 977 unit tests, lint and `npm run verify` pass.

- **2026-07-18 (z)** — _`Toast` becomes a real §2.5 primitive; closes issue #9. No schema, DDL, store or sequencer behaviour changed, and no toast the user sees is worded or timed differently._ **(1) The gap was structural.** §2.5 names Toast in the bespoke primitive set, but `src/ui/primitives/` shipped every member of that list except it — toasts were drawn inline by `ui/ToastViewport.tsx`, whose header still promised "the full Toast primitive lands in Phase 7". Phase 7 had shipped. Toasts are the app-wide feedback surface for autosave (§4.4), import/export, bounce and the §9.7 eviction warning, so the one notice type with no primitive was also the most widely raised, which is exactly the §3.6 call-site-restyling risk that rule exists to prevent. **(2) The split follows the §3.6 line: the primitive owns a notice, the viewport owns the queue.** `primitives/Toast.tsx` carries the tone variants, the dismiss affordance and the entry/exit motion; `ToastViewport` is reduced to the fixed placement container and the mapping from `useUIStore.toasts`. **(3) Severity→role derivation moved into the primitive rather than staying at the mount site.** info/success announce politely (`status`), warning/error interrupt (`alert`) — §8.2, unchanged behaviour — but a caller can no longer render a notice with a tone and a role that disagree, because it never supplies the role. The §11.4 smoke's `data-testid="toast"` / `data-tone` seam moves with the markup and is untouched. **(4) Motion is new, and is the one visible difference.** Notices previously appeared and vanished instantly; they now fade and rise 12 px on entry and reverse on exit, transform/opacity only (§8.3), collapsing to a plain opacity fade under `prefers-reduced-motion` via `useReducedMotion`, matching `Modal`. This required the viewport's `toasts.length === 0` early return to go — `AnimatePresence` cannot play an exit from an unmounted parent — so the container is now always mounted; it is `pointer-events-none` and empty, so it intercepts nothing. **(5) Four `it.each` cases pin the tone→role mapping** in `primitives.test.tsx` alongside the other §8.2 contract tests, where previously nothing asserted it at the component level. Suite 917 tests, all passing.

- **2026-07-18 (y)** — _The §8.5.4 worker-computed peak pyramid is implemented and the §8.5.7 Browser waveform micro-preview built on it; closes issue #8 and lifts the Phase 7 deferral recorded in item (5) of (h). No schema, DDL, store or sequencer behaviour changed._ **(1) The pyramid had to come first, which is why #8 waited.** Browser lists a whole library directory, so drawing a thumbnail per row by decoding each sample would put hundreds of decodes on the main thread — the §3.3 violation the issue predicted. `peakPyramid.ts` (pure) reduces a mono mix to min/max buckets and coarsens by folding pairs, `peakPyramid.worker.ts` runs it over a sample's canonical WAV bytes off the main thread, and `peakPyramidCache.ts` memoises one pyramid per OPFS path so every surface that draws a sample shares one reduction. `WaveformCanvas` now takes a pyramid rather than a `Float32Array` and maps the cheapest sufficient level onto its own pixel columns, so drawing costs the same for a one-shot as for a ten-minute sample. **(2) Level 0's bucket _count_ is capped, not its bucket _size_ — a fixed size is wrong at both ends.** The first implementation used a fixed 256 frames, which for a 5760-frame maracas one-shot gave 23 buckets: stretched across a ~1000 px editor canvas that drew as a visible staircase, a fidelity regression against the old code, which drew one sample per column. The size is now derived as `ceil(frames / MAX_BASE_BUCKETS)` with `MAX_BASE_BUCKETS = 8192`, so short samples get a bucket per frame and stay smooth, long ones stay bounded — which also caps a cached pyramid at ~128 KiB, where a fixed size would have made a ten-minute sample's level 0 alone ~450 KiB. **(3) There is deliberately no `invalidate`.** A destructive edit renders a NEW OPFS file with a new `sampleId` (§8.5.4) rather than rewriting one in place, so a path's audio never changes under a cached pyramid and a §8.5.4-style "recompute after destructive edits" hook would be an orphan (§3.4). Memory is bounded by an LRU cap (96 pyramids) instead, since scrolling a large library would otherwise accumulate one per sample ever seen. **(4) The micro-preview loads on visibility, not on render.** `SampleWaveformThumb` requests its pyramid from an `IntersectionObserver` with a 128 px margin, so a directory of hundreds of samples reduces only what the user has scrolled to; environments without the observer (jsdom) load eagerly, correctness over laziness. It is `aria-hidden` — a new `decorative` prop on `WaveformCanvas` — because the row already announces the sample name and a per-row image role would only add noise to the §8.2 reading. **(5) `SampleEditPanel` dropped its local `monoOf`** in favour of the shared `monoDownmix`, and takes its Trim bounds from `pyramid.frames` rather than a decoded buffer's length — the panel no longer decodes the sample to draw it at all.

- **2026-07-18 (x)** — _A pitch bend arriving while a pitch envelope or mono glide is still running now renders, by summing into the voice's pitch on its own node instead of competing for the same `AudioParam` timeline. Settles the design question (w) item (4) left open — bends **sum** with programmed pitch, they do not override it — and adds one §10.2 rule. No schema, DDL, store or sequencer behaviour changed; no new constant._ **(1) The defect, recorded by (w) item (4) and fixed here.** `VoicePool.retune` wrote the retune onto `source.detune` with `setTargetAtTime`, which Web Audio renders only while it is the _last_ automation event on that param. A voice built with a pitch envelope, or a keygroup glide's `setValueAtTime` + `linearRampToValueAtTime`, already has later events queued, and a pending linear ramp interpolates from the value at the _preceding_ event's time — so the bend never rendered at all, and once the contour's final event passed the param sat at the contour's own end value, losing the bend permanently rather than merely delaying it. Both entry points were affected: `applyProgramDetune` (§10.2 bend) and `applyPadParam('detune', …)` (§6 live edit / §7.8 automation). Not affected, and deliberately unchanged: the filter envelope is scheduled on `filter.detune` while `applyPadParam('filterFrequency')` ramps `filter.frequency` — different params, no collision. **(2) Sum, not override — the design decision.** §6 and §10.2 did not say which should win. Summing is now the rule (§10.2), because a bend is a _performance gesture applied over_ the programmed sound, exactly as §6 already layered it over pad tune and static pitch mod; an envelope or glide is part of the patch. Overriding would mean bending a gliding note cancels its portamento and bending mid-envelope truncates the sound design, which is not what either control means. Of the three candidates, `cancelAndHoldAtTime` before the retune (cheap, no new node) was rejected for exactly that reason, and re-scheduling the remaining contour offset by the bend was rejected as re-deriving the envelope's remaining points on every bend for no behavioural gain over summing. **(3) The bend rides a `ConstantSourceNode`.** Each voice gets one whose `offset` carries the bend in cents, connected into `source.detune`. Node inputs _sum_ with a param's automation rather than competing with it, so the bend applies cleanly during an envelope or glide and survives afterwards — which is the whole reason this works where writing the param did not. It is built lazily on the first retune, not at note-on, so a voice that is never bent costs no extra node; `teardown` stops and disconnects it. Bend and pad detune share the one offset, so the later of the two supersedes the other — the same mutual clobbering the old absolute writes had, preserved deliberately. **(4) `detuneSchedule.ts`: the bend is now an additive track.** `DetuneSchedule` gains a mutable `bend` breakpoint list beside the fixed `breakpoints` and `oscillations`, mirroring the fact that the voice keeps the two on separate params, and `baseDetuneAt` returns their sum. The bend track holds _zero_ before its first point rather than holding that point's value, since the node contributes nothing until a bend arrives; the base contour keeps its existing hold-the-first-value rule. `applyRetune` therefore appends a terminate-then-step pair to `bend` instead of rewriting `breakpoints`, and its former no-op guard — which faithfully modelled the defect — is gone. `segmentBoundaries` merges corners from both tracks so no integrated span crosses one; duplicate times are harmless, since a zero-length span integrates to nothing. The declick integration is unchanged in kind and stays exact. **(5) Test-mock extension.** `src/test/mocks/audioContext.ts` gains `createConstantSource` with an `offset` param recording calls like the others, and `FakeAudioNode` now records node→**param** connections in `paramOutputs` (previously dropped on the floor), so a test can assert the bend node is actually summed into `source.detune` — `liveNodeCount` cannot see it, because a node connected only to a param has no node outputs. **(6) Tests.** The two that asserted the defective behaviour were rewritten to assert the new rule rather than deleted: `detuneSchedule.test.ts` "ignores a retune superseded by a ramp that is still pending" → "sums a retune onto a contour ramp that is still pending", and `voicePool.test.ts` "does not move the declick for a bend a pending pitch ramp will swallow" → "applies a bend that arrives while a pitch envelope ramp is still pending", whose end time (1.4293 s, hand-integrated) replaces the unbent 0.7787 s the fade wrongly kept. Added: a stepping-between-successive-retunes case, a test that the bend still stands after the envelope's last event and is torn down with the voice, and one asserting an un-retuned voice builds no bend node.
- **2026-07-18 (w)** — _The end-of-buffer declick is now integrated from the voice's whole detune contour, closing the modulator half of the (t) item (5) limitation and all of issue #87. Directed by the human developer, who chose option 2 from that issue over the standing "leave it". No schema, DDL, store or sequencer behaviour changed; no new constant._ **(1) What (v) left open.** (v) made the declick exact for a _retuned_ voice by banking piecewise-constant rate segments, but three §6 features vary `source.detune` inside the AudioParam where the pool never observed it — the pitch envelope, keygroup mono glide, and a pitch-routed LFO. For those voices the fade stayed a base-rate estimate, landing early (a truncated tail) or late (a reduced but audible click). Both (v) item (5) and #87 recorded option 2 as rejected on the grounds that it duplicates the modulation maths; that rejection is **superseded** — the maths is not duplicated, because the contour is now described once and both consumed by the AudioParam writes and read by the integrator. **(2) `core/audio/detuneSchedule.ts` (new).** A voice consumes buffer at `2^(detune/1200)` seconds per second, so its true end is where `∫ 2^(detune(τ)/1200) dτ` equals the region length. The module models the contour as piecewise-linear breakpoints (what the pool writes to `source.detune`) plus the oscillators summed onto it (what LFO pitch routes connect), and solves that integral with the midpoint rule. `modEnvelopeBreakpoints` in `voiceEnvelope.ts` is the **single description of the AHDSR shape** — `scheduleModEnvelope` writes those points to the param and the integrator reads the same points, so the model and the sound cannot drift. Glide and static-detune contours are built the same way in `buildVoice`, and `wireLfos` now returns descriptors of the pitch-routed oscillators it wired. **(3) Cost is bounded, and does not scale with sample length.** Sloped segments are subdivided so the base moves at most 10 cents per step; oscillating spans get 32 steps per cycle of the fastest LFO. Once the base has settled the tail is advanced a whole LFO period at a time, so only a final partial period is stepped — a five-minute pad costs the same as a one-shot. Step counts are capped (8192 per span), which trades resolution rather than time in the pathological case of a 0.01 Hz LFO against a 100 Hz one. Two LFOs on pitch are advanced by the period that leaves both closest to a whole number of cycles, searched over 32 cycles of the slower; an exact common period is the normal result, and a pair with no small one is necessarily far enough apart that the leftover phase averages out well inside the 3 ms fade. **(4) A Web Audio semantic this work depended on getting right, worth not rediscovering.** The pool retunes with `setTargetAtTime`, which renders only while it is the _last_ automation event on the param. A voice whose pitch envelope or glide ramp is still pending already has later events queued, and a pending `linearRampToValueAtTime` interpolates from the value at the preceding event's time — so **a bend arriving mid-envelope never renders at all**, and is lost for good once the envelope's final event passes. `applyRetune` models exactly that (it is a no-op while a ramp is pending) rather than assuming the retune lands, because assuming it would put the fade somewhere the voice never goes. This is pre-existing §10.2 behaviour, unchanged here and now recorded rather than fixed; the fix is a §6 question about whether a bend should override a pitch envelope, not a §5.4 declick question. **Superseded by (x)**, which settled that question — bends sum — and fixed it by moving the bend onto its own summing node, so `applyRetune` no longer models a swallowed retune. **(5) Unchanged from (v):** the retune path, the `cancelAndHoldAtTime(declickFadeStart)` re-lay, and the two cases deliberately left alone (a region already run out, and a fade already in flight). Covered by 12 tests in `detuneSchedule.test.ts` — several checking the solver against an independent brute-force integration at a far finer step — plus four in `voicePool.test.ts` driving the envelope, glide, LFO and swallowed-bend paths end to end.
- **2026-07-18 (v)** — _The end-of-buffer declick now follows a retuned voice, closing the pitch-bend half of the (t) item (5) limitation (issue #87). No schema, DDL, store or sequencer behaviour changed; no new constant._ **(1) What was wrong.** (t) laid the declick once, at note-on, from the voice's base detune. But `applyProgramDetune` (§10.2 pitch bend) and `applyPadParam('detune', …)` (§6 live edit / automation) both retune a _sounding_ voice, and detune is the playback rate — so a bend held through the end of a sample moves the moment the buffer actually runs out, away from a fade already scheduled for the old one. Bend down an octave halfway through a one-second sample and the voice sounds for 1.5 s while the fade sits at 1.0 s: the amp reaches zero half a second early and the tail is lost. **(2) The pool now tracks consumption instead of assuming a rate.** A voice carries `regionSeconds`, `consumedSeconds`, `rateSince` and `rate`; each retune banks `(now − rateSince) × rate` of buffer as spent and divides what is left by the new rate to get the true end. Rate is therefore integrated as piecewise-constant segments, which is exact for any number of successive bends. The `PARAM_RAMP_MS` dezipper on `detune` is treated as instantaneous — 10 ms of ramp is far shorter than the error being corrected. **(3) Re-laying the fade erases the stale one first.** `rescheduleDeclick` calls `cancelAndHoldAtTime(declickFadeStart)` before scheduling the new fade, which both removes the old ramp and leaves the amp on whatever level the AHDSR had reached there — exactly the level the new fade wants to depart from. Holding at the _new_ start instead would be wrong whenever the retune pushes the end later, because the old ramp would already have run to zero and the extra tail would play silent. **(4) Two cases are deliberately left alone**, because re-laying could only make them worse: a region that has already run out, and a fade already in flight (`when >= declickFadeStart`) — the ramp underway is nearer the truth than anything scheduled behind it, and cutting it short would reintroduce the click. The worst case is a bend arriving inside the last 3 ms, where the fade stays where (t) put it. **(5) The rest of item (5) stands.** A pitch envelope, keygroup glide or pitch-routed LFO varies `detune` _inside_ the AudioParam, where the pool cannot observe it, so those voices keep an estimated end time. Option 2 from #87 — integrating the envelope/LFO maths outside the AudioParam — remains rejected: it duplicates the modulation maths and still could not see a bend arriving mid-note, which is the case that actually mattered.
